\bm{r}
the composition

\operatorname{Tot}^{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{1}\times\mathfrak{M}_{2}\times\mathfrak{M}_{3}}(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{1,2})\rightarrow\operatorname{Tot}^{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{1}\times\mathfrak{M}_{2}}(\mathcal{R}^{\bullet})\xrightarrow{\bm{q}}\mathfrak{M}_{P_{1}(t)+P_{2}(t)}.

Via the chain of quasi-equivalences
\pi^{\prime*}_{1,2,4}\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}\rightarrow r^{*}\mathcal{U}\leftarrow r^{*}\mathcal{L}^{\bullet}
we deduce that there is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{D}(\operatorname{Tot}^{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{1}\times\mathfrak{M}_{2}\times\mathfrak{M}_{3}}(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{1,2}))
:

\bm{\delta}^{\prime*}\!\left(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{2,3}\oplus\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{1,3}\right)\cong\mathcal{H}om(\bm{q}^{*}\mathcal{L}^{\bullet},\pi_{3,4}^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}^{\bullet}).

Along with Proposition
4.13
, this isomorphism yields the part of the final statement of Assumption
3
concerning
\bm{\alpha}^{\prime}
. The argument dealing with
\bm{\beta}^{\prime}
is identical. Putting everything together:

Proposition 6.11
.

The stack
\mathfrak{Coh}^{\sst}_{p(t)}(S)
satisfies Assumption
3
.

6.1.6.
Good moduli spaces

The existence of a good moduli space
\mathtt{JH}\colon\mathfrak{Coh}^{\sst}_{P(t)}(X)\to\mathcal{C}\!oh^{\sst}_{P(t)}(X)
is well-known and can be deduced from Alper’s original paper on good moduli spaces
[
1
, §13]
and the standard GIT construction of moduli spaces of sheaves on projective varieties as in
[
69
]
. The stack
\mathfrak{Coh}^{\sst}_{p(t)}(X)
also admits a good moduli space, because it is a disjoint union of stacks of the form
\mathfrak{Coh}^{\sst}_{P(t)}(X)
. Thus we have

Proposition 6.12
.

The stack
\mathfrak{Coh}^{\sst}_{p(t)}(X)
satisfies Assumption
4
.

6.1.7.
The quasi-projective case

If
X
is a quasi-projective variety, we let
X\subseteq\overline{X}
be a projective compactification. We fix an ample divisor of
\overline{X}
and for
P(t)\in\mathbf{Q}[t]
, define
\mathfrak{Coh}_{P(t)}^{\sst}(X)\subseteq\mathfrak{Coh}_{P(t)}^{\sst}(\overline{X})
to be the open substack of sheaves with support avoiding
\overline{X}\smallsetminus X
. Since Assumptions
1
-
3
are stable under base change along open inclusions, we can generalise the above discussion with the following

Proposition 6.13
.

Let
X
be a smooth quasi-projective variety. Then the moduli stack
\mathfrak{Coh}_{p(t)}^{\sst}(X)
satisfies Assumptions
1
and
4
. If
X=S
is a surface, then
\mathfrak{Coh}_{p(t)}^{\sst}(S)
additionally satisfies Assumptions
2
and
3
.

Proof.

It remains to check Assumption
4
. For the good moduli spaces
\mathtt{JH}\colon\mathfrak{Coh}^{\sst}_{P(t)}(\overline{X})\to\mathcal{C}\!oh^{\sst}_{P(t)}(\overline{X})
we have
\mathtt{JH}^{-1}(\mathtt{JH}(\mathfrak{Coh}^{\sst}_{P(t)}(X)))=\mathfrak{Coh}^{\sst}_{P(t)}(X)
(i.e.
\mathfrak{Coh}^{\sst}_{P(t)}(X)\subseteq\mathfrak{Coh}^{\sst}_{P(t)}(\overline{X})
is
saturated
in the sense of
[
1
]
). Hence the good moduli space
\mathfrak{Coh}_{P(t)}^{\sst}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\!oh_{P(t)}^{\sst}(X)
is obtained from
\mathfrak{Coh}_{P(t)}^{\sst}(\overline{X})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\!oh_{P(t)}^{\sst}(\overline{X})
by restriction.
∎

6.1.8.
Finiteness of
\oplus

Recall that one of the assumptions in the construction of the BPS Lie algebra is the finiteness of the direct sum map
\oplus
on the good moduli space. For a reduced Hilbert polynomial
p(t)\in\mathbf{Q}[t]/\sim
we let
\mathcal{C}\!oh^{\sst}_{p(t)}(X)
be the disjoint union of all
\mathcal{C}\!oh^{\sst}_{P(t)}(X)
with
P(t)
either zero, or in the equivalence class
p(t)
.

Proposition 6.14
.

Let
X
be a quasiprojective variety, with a fixed ample divisor. Then,

\oplus\colon\mathcal{C}\!oh^{\sst}_{p(t)}(X)\times\mathcal{C}\!oh^{\sst}_{p(t)}(X)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\!oh^{\sst}_{p(t)}(X)

is finite, and thus proper. In particular, the morphism
\oplus
satisfies Assumption
5
from §
5.1
.

Proof.

The fibre of a point
y
parametrising a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
under the morphism
\oplus
is identified with the set of pairs
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime})
for which there is an isomorphism
\mathcal{F}\cong\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\oplus\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}
. Since the category of semistable coherent sheaves on
X
with fixed normalised Hilbert polynomial is of finite length, this fibre is finite. So the proposition boils down to proving properness.

If
X=\overline{X}
properness is immediate, since each connected component of the the variety
\mathcal{C}\!oh^{\sst}_{p(t)}(X)
is itself projective. For the general case, we use the valuative criterion for properness. Denote by
\overline{\oplus}
the extension of
\oplus
to the direct sum morphism for
\mathcal{C}\!oh^{\sst}_{p(t)}(\overline{X})
; we have just seen that this morphism is finite. Denote by
R
a discrete valuation ring with residue field
k
, and by
K
its field of fractions. Then given the diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{Spec}(K)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\!oh^{\sst}_{p(t)}(X)\times\mathcal{C}\!oh^{\sst}_{p(t)}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\oplus}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\!oh^{\sst}_{p(t)}(\overline{X})\times\mathcal{C}\!oh^{\sst}_{p(t)}(\overline{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\oplus}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spec}(k)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spec}(R)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{l}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\!oh^{\sst}_{p(t)}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\!oh^{\sst}_{p(t)}(\overline{X})}

there is a unique morphism
\mathrm{Spec}(R)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\!oh^{\sst}_{p(t)}(\overline{X})\times\mathcal{M}^{\sst}_{p(t)}(\overline{X})
making the whole diagram commute. This lift factors through
j
if the induced morphism
\alpha\colon\mathrm{Spec}(k)\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\!oh^{\sst}_{p(t)}(\overline{X})\times\mathcal{C}\!oh^{\sst}_{p(t)}(\overline{X})
does. But this morphism corresponds to a direct sum decomposition of the coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
corresponding to the morphism
j^{\prime}lr
. Since this morphism factors through
j^{\prime}
, the sheaf
\mathcal{F}
is supported on
X
, and so in any direct sum decomposition
\mathcal{F}\cong\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\oplus\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}
both
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}
are supported on
X
. It follows that
\alpha
factors through
j
.
∎

For
S
a smooth quasi-projective surface, the only remaining assumption that will go into the construction of the BPS Lie algebra is Assumption
6
. In order to satisfy this assumption we will either restrict to coherent sheaves on smooth quasi-projective
S
with
\mathcal{O}_{S}\cong K_{S}
, or consider coherent sheaves on smooth quasi-projective
S
with zero-dimensional support.

6.2.
Algebra

For categories of representations over algebras, checking that the Assumptions
1
-
5
of §
5
hold is much more straightforward. Nonetheless, we spell out some of the details below.

6.2.1.
A class of quiver algebras

Let
B
be an algebra, and let us assume that we have presented
B
in the form

(6.2)

\displaystyle B=A/\langle R\rangle.

Here,
A
is the universal localisation (as in
[
66
]
) of the free path algebra of a quiver
kQ
, obtained by formally inverting a finite set of elements
b_{1},\ldots,b_{l}
, where each
b_{i}
is a linear combination of cyclic paths with the same start-points. We assume that
R=\{r_{1},\ldots,r_{e}\}
is a finite set of relations in
A
, and without loss of generality we assume that each
r_{i}
is a linear combination of paths with the same starting point, and the same ending point. We define the dimension vector
(M_{i}\coloneqq e_{i}\cdot M)_{i\in Q_{0}}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}
of a
B
-module
M
in the usual way.

6.2.2.
Good moduli spaces

For
\mathbf{d}\in Q_{0}
the stack
\mathfrak{M}_{B,\mathbf{d}}
of
\mathbf{d}
-dimensional
B
-modules is a global quotient of an affine scheme, and thus satisfies the resolution property. The coarse moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{B,\mathbf{d}}
is the affinization of
\mathfrak{M}_{B,\mathbf{d}}
, and the morphism

\mathtt{JH}\colon\mathfrak{M}_{B,\mathbf{d}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{B,\mathbf{d}}

is a good moduli space for
\mathfrak{M}_{B,\mathbf{d}}
, i.e.
\mathfrak{M}_{B}
satisfies Assumption
4
. The following is proved in exactly the same way as Proposition
2.2
:

Proposition 6.15
.

The direct sum map
\oplus\colon\mathcal{M}_{B}\times\mathcal{M}_{B}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{B}
is finite. In particular, the category of finite-dimensional
B
-modules satisfies Assumption
5
.

We have shown that the geometric Assumptions
4
and
5
in §
5.3
that go into the definition of the BPS algebra are always met for
B\lmod
. In the rest of this section we check Assumptions
1
-
3
.

6.2.3.
Properness

Base changing from the stack of
Q
-representations also deals with Assumption
1
:

Proposition 6.16
.

Let
\mathfrak{Exact}_{B}
denote the stack of short exact sequences of finite-dimensional
B
-modules. Then the forgetful morphism
p\colon\mathfrak{Exact}_{B}\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}_{B}
taking a short exact sequence to its central term is a projective representable morphism of stacks. In particular, the category of finite-dimensional
B
-modules satisfies Assumption
1
.

Proof.

We have a Cartesian square of stacks

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Exact}_{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}_{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Exact}_{kQ}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}_{kQ}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

in which
p^{\prime}
is easily shown to be representable and proper. The result follows by base change.
∎

6.2.4.
Derived enhancement of
B

Our explicit presentation of
B
determines a derived enhancement of it. If
R=\{r_{1},\ldots,r_{e}\}
is a finite set of relations on the localised path algebra
A
, and each
r_{i}
is a linear combination of paths with the same starting point and ending point, we first define the graded quiver
\widetilde{Q}
by setting

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{0}

\displaystyle=Q_{0}

\displaystyle(\widetilde{Q}_{1})^{0}

\displaystyle=Q_{1}

\displaystyle(\widetilde{Q}_{1})^{-1}

\displaystyle=x_{1},\ldots,x_{e}

\displaystyle(\widetilde{Q}_{1})^{i}

\displaystyle=\varnothing

\displaystyle\textrm{if }i\neq 0,-1.

We set
t(x_{i})=t(r_{i})
and
s(x_{i})=s(r_{i})
. Then we localise
k\widetilde{Q}
with respect to the same localising set as
A
, and denote by
\widetilde{A}
the resulting graded algebra. Finally we set
\widetilde{B}=(\widetilde{A},d)
, where
d
is determined by the Leibniz rule and by setting
d(x_{i})=r_{i}
. Then
\widetilde{B}
is a dga, concentrated in nonpositive degrees, with
\HO^{0}(\widetilde{B})\cong B
, which we call the
derived enhancement
of
B
. We remark that
\widetilde{B}
depends on a presentation of
B
, and not just
B
itself. We will be particularly interested in examples in which the natural morphism of dgas
\widetilde{B}\rightarrow B
is a quasi-isomorphism.

Example 6.17
.

For
Q
a quiver,
\overline{Q}
its double,
A=k\overline{Q}
and
R=\{e_{i}\sum_{a\in Q_{1}}[a,a^{*}]e_{i}\;\lvert\;i\in Q_{0}\}
,
B=\Pi_{Q}
is the preprojective algebra, while
\widetilde{B}=\mathscr{G}_{2}(Q)
is the derived preprojective algebra.

6.2.5.
Bimodule resolution

Consider the complex of
\widetilde{A}
-bimodules

0\rightarrow\ker(m^{\prime})\xrightarrow{i}\bigoplus_{i\in Q_{0}}\widetilde{A}e_{i}\otimes e_{i}\widetilde{A}\xrightarrow{m^{\prime}}\widetilde{A}\rightarrow 0.

This is in fact a double complex, since each term carries an internal differential induced by
d
.

Lemma 6.18
.

There is an isomorphism of
\widetilde{A}
-bimodules

\ker(m^{\prime})\cong\bigoplus_{a\in\widetilde{Q}_{1}}\widetilde{A}e_{t(a)}\otimes e_{s(a)}\widetilde{A}

and under this isomorphism
i
is sent to the morphism
\iota
sending
e_{t(a)}\otimes e_{s(a)}
to
a\otimes 1-1\otimes a
.

We introduce some preparation for the proof of this lemma.

Definition 6.19
.

An algebra
C
is called
formally smooth
if and only if it satisfies the two equivalent properties

(1)

For any algebra
D
with two-sided nilpotent ideal
I
, any morphism
C\rightarrow D/I
lifts to a morphism
C\rightarrow D
.

(2)

The bimodule
\ker(C\otimes C\xrightarrow{m}C)
is a projective
C
-bimodule.

Lemma 6.20
.

Let
C
be a formally smooth algebra, and let
C^{\prime}
be the universal localisation at a set of elements
c_{1},\ldots,c_{i}
. Then
C^{\prime}
is formally smooth.

Proof.

By the universal property of universal localisation, for any algebra
D
there is an embedding

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathrm{Alg}}(C^{\prime},D)\subseteq\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathrm{Alg}}(C,D)

as the subset of homomorphisms sending
c_{i}
to units in
D
. Let
C^{\prime}
be as in the statement of the Lemma. Let
f\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathrm{Alg}}(C^{\prime},D/I)
. It is sufficient to show that
f
lifts to a homomorphism in
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathrm{Alg}}(C^{\prime},D/I^{2})
. By formal smoothness of
C
there is a
g\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathrm{Alg}}(C,D/I^{2})
inducing
f
under restriction. By assumption, for each
c_{j}
there is a
d\in D/I^{2}
such that
g(c_{j})d=1+n
, where
n\in I
. But then
g(c_{j})d(1-n)=1+n^{\prime}
where
n^{\prime}\in I^{2}
, and so
g\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathrm{Alg}}(C^{\prime},D/I^{2})
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
6.18
.

For the purposes of the proof, we may forget the cohomological grading on
\widetilde{A}
, and we denote the resulting ungraded algebra
\widetilde{A}_{\ungr}
. For the special case
\widetilde{A}_{\ungr}=k\widetilde{Q}_{\ungr}
it is known that

0\rightarrow\bigoplus_{a\in\widetilde{Q}_{1}}k\widetilde{Q}_{\ungr}e_{t(a)}\otimes e_{s(a)}k\widetilde{Q}_{\ungr}\xrightarrow{\iota^{\prime}}\bigoplus_{i\in Q_{0}}k\widetilde{Q}_{\ungr}e_{i}\otimes e_{i}k\widetilde{Q}_{\ungr}\xrightarrow{m^{\prime}}k\widetilde{Q}_{\ungr}\rightarrow 0

is exact. Since

\ker(m)\cong\ker(m^{\prime})\oplus\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\in Q_{0}\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}k\widetilde{Q}_{\ungr}e_{i}\otimes e_{j}k\widetilde{Q}_{\ungr}

we deduce that
k\widetilde{Q}_{\ungr}
is formally smooth, and so
\widetilde{A}
is by Lemma
6.20
.

Since the image of
\iota
lies in the kernel of
m
, and
\widetilde{A}_{\ungr}\otimes_{k\widetilde{Q}_{\ungr}}-
and
-\otimes_{k\widetilde{Q}_{\ungr}}\widetilde{A}_{\ungr}
are right exact, we obtain the split surjection of projective bimodules

p\colon\bigoplus_{a\in\widetilde{Q}_{1}}\widetilde{A}_{\ungr}e_{t(a)}\otimes e_{s(a)}\widetilde{A}_{\ungr}\rightarrow\ker(m^{\prime}).

Each of the summands appearing in the domain are indecomposable: since each summand
P
is a cyclic
\widetilde{A}\otimes\widetilde{A}^{\mathrm{op}}
-module, an endomorphism is provided by an element
p=p^{\prime}\otimes p^{\prime\prime}\in P
, which is a projection if and only if
p^{\prime}p^{\prime}=p^{\prime}
and
p^{\prime\prime}p^{\prime\prime}=p^{\prime\prime}
: this forces
p^{\prime}=e_{t(a)}
and
p^{\prime\prime}=e_{s(a)}
. We deduce that there is a subset
\Omega\subseteq\widetilde{Q}_{1}
such that

\bigoplus_{a\in\Omega}\widetilde{A}_{\ungr}e_{t(a)}\otimes e_{s(a)}\widetilde{A}_{\ungr}\cong\ker(m^{\prime}).

By assumption we localised at linear combinations of cyclic paths with the same start points, hence for every
i\in Q_{0}
there is an
\widetilde{A}
-module
N_{i}
of dimension
1_{i}
. Let
i,j\in Q_{0}
. Then we may calculate
\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{k\widetilde{Q}_{\ungr}}(N_{i},N_{j})
as the
n
th cohomology of the Hom complex

\operatorname{Hom}\left(\left(\bigoplus_{a\in\widetilde{Q}_{1}}k\widetilde{Q}_{\ungr}e_{t(a)}\otimes e_{s(a)}k\widetilde{Q}_{\ungr}\xrightarrow{\iota^{\prime}}\bigoplus_{i\in Q_{0}}k\widetilde{Q}_{\ungr}e_{i}\otimes e_{i}k\widetilde{Q}_{\ungr}\right)\otimes_{k\widetilde{Q}_{\ungr}}N_{i},N_{j}\right)

and we find that
\operatorname{ext}^{1}_{k\widetilde{Q}_{\ungr}}(N_{i},N_{j})
is the number of arrows
i\rightarrow j
. The same calculation gives that
\operatorname{ext}^{1}_{\widetilde{A}}(N_{i},N_{j})
is the number of such arrows belonging to
\Omega
. By
[
66
, Thm.4.7]
we have the equality
\operatorname{ext}^{1}_{k\widetilde{Q}_{\ungr}}(N_{i},N_{j})=\operatorname{ext}^{1}_{\widetilde{A}}(N_{i},N_{j})
and so
\Omega=\widetilde{Q}_{1}
and the result follows.
∎

We consider
\ker(m^{\prime})\xrightarrow{i}\bigoplus_{i\in Q_{0}}\widetilde{B}e_{i}\otimes e_{i}\widetilde{B}\xrightarrow{m^{\prime}}\widetilde{B}
as a double complex, with the differential
d
on
\widetilde{B}
inducing the second differential. This double complex has exact rows by Lemma
6.18
, the total complex of the double complex of
\widetilde{B}
-modules
\ker(m^{\prime})\xrightarrow{i}\widetilde{B}\otimes\widetilde{B}
provides a resolution of the diagonal bimodule
\widetilde{B}
by perfect
\widetilde{B}
-bimodules. The following corollaries of this construction are immediate.

Corollary 6.21
.

There is a fully faithful embedding of categories
B\lmod\rightarrow\operatorname{Perf}(\widetilde{B})
from the category of finite-dimensional
B
-modules to the perfect derived category of
\widetilde{B}
-modules.

Proof.

If
M
is a finite-dimensional
B
-module, we may consider it as a finite-dimensional
\widetilde{B}
-module via the canonical morphism
\widetilde{B}\rightarrow\HO^{0}(\widetilde{B})\cong B
. Then

\left(\ker(m^{\prime})\xrightarrow{i}\bigoplus_{i\in Q_{0}}\widetilde{B}e_{i}\otimes e_{i}\widetilde{B}\right)\otimes_{\widetilde{B}}M\rightarrow\widetilde{B}\otimes_{\widetilde{B}}M

provides a resolution of
M
by perfect
\widetilde{B}
-modules.
∎

Let
\pi\colon\ker(m)\rightarrow\ker(m^{\prime})
be the natural projection. For the next corollary, we first define

\displaystyle D\colon

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}\rightarrow\ker(m^{\prime})

\displaystyle\alpha\mapsto\pi(\alpha\otimes 1-1\otimes\alpha).

Given an element
\alpha\otimes\alpha^{\prime}\in\widetilde{A}e_{t(a)}\otimes e_{s(a)}\widetilde{A}
,
\widetilde{A}
-modules
M,N
, and a homomorphism
f_{a}\colon\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(N_{s(a)},M_{t(a)})
we define the evaluation
(M,f,N)(\alpha\otimes\alpha^{\prime})=M(\alpha)\circ f_{a}\circ N(\alpha^{\prime})
.

Corollary 6.22
.

Let
M
and
N
be two finite-dimensional
B
-modules. Then the cohomology of the complex

(6.3)

\displaystyle 0\leftarrow\bigoplus_{r\in R}\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(M_{s(r)},N_{t(r)})\xleftarrow{\alpha}\bigoplus_{a\in Q_{1}}\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(M_{s(a)},N_{t(a)})\xleftarrow{\beta}\bigoplus_{i\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{Hom}_{k}(M_{i},M_{j})\leftarrow 0

calculates
\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\widetilde{B}}(M,N)
, where we define

\displaystyle\alpha((f_{a})_{a\in Q_{1}})

\displaystyle=((M,f,N)(Dr))_{r\in R}

\displaystyle\beta((f_{i})_{i\in Q_{0}})

\displaystyle=(N(a)f_{s(a)}-f_{t(a)}M(a))_{a\in Q_{1}}.

In particular, Assumption
2
holds for
\mathfrak{M}_{B}
, considered as a substack of
t_{0}(\bm{\mathfrak{M}}_{\widetilde{B}})
.

Remark 6.23
.

If
B=\HO^{0}(\widetilde{B})
it follows that the complex (
6.3
) calculates
\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{B}(M,N)
.

The following proposition is a result of the well-known explicit presentation of
\mathfrak{Filt}
over
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)},\mathbf{d}^{(3)}}
. Since its proof is strictly easier than the analogous statement for coherent sheaves, provided in §
6.1
, we omit it.

Proposition 6.24
.

Assumption
3
holds for the stack
\mathfrak{M}_{B}
.

In conclusion,
all
of the geometric assumptions (Assumptions
1
-
3
) from §
5.2
that will be required in §
9.1
to define the CoHA structure on (an appropriate shift of)
\mathtt{JH}_{*}\mathbb{D}k_{\mathfrak{M}_{B,\mathbf{d}}}
hold for arbitrary
B
presented in the general form (
6.2
). Moreover, the geometric conditions (Assumptions
4
and
5
) in §
5.3
are also met. So as long as
\widetilde{B}
is a 2-Calabi–Yau algebra, we may define the BPS algebra for
B\lmod
as in §
10
below.

7.
Totally negative
2
-Calabi–Yau categories

In this paper, we are interested in certain
2
-Calabi–Yau Abelian categories
\mathcal{A}
that arise in the general context described in §
5.1
-§
5.3
, which we call
totally negative
. Namely, we say that
\mathcal{A}
is
totally negative
if for any pair of nonzero objects
M,N
of
\mathcal{A}
,
(M,N)_{\mathcal{A}}<0
.

7.1.
The preprojective algebra of a totally negative quiver

Let
Q=(Q_{0},Q_{1})
be a quiver. Following Bozec–Schiffmann
[
9
, Section 1.2]
, we say that
Q
is
totally negative
if its symmetrised Euler form
(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{e})_{\Pi_{Q}}=\langle\mathbf{d},\mathbf{e}\rangle_{Q}+\langle\mathbf{e},\mathbf{d}\rangle_{Q}
is totally negative, that is
(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{e})_{\Pi_{Q}}<0
for any
\mathbf{d},\mathbf{e}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}\smallsetminus\{0\}
. Unraveling the definition of the Euler form, this is equivalent to the requirement that any vertex of
Q
carries at least two loops and any two vertices are connected by at least one arrow. The category of representations of
\Pi_{Q}
is then a totally negative
2
-Calabi–Yau category, as its Euler form is
(-,-)_{\Pi_{Q}}
. These totally negative
2
-Calabi–Yau categories constitute the building blocks of more general totally negative
2
-Calabi–Yau categories, by the local neighbourhood theorem (Theorem
5.3
) and the following proposition.

Proposition 7.1
.

The Ext-quiver of any
\Sigma
-collection (§
5.4.4
) in a totally negative
2
-Calabi–Yau Abelian category
\mathcal{A}
is the double of a totally negative quiver.

Proof.

Let
\underline{\mathcal{F}}=\{\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{r}\}
be a
\Sigma
-collection in
\mathcal{A}
and let
\overline{Q}
be the Ext-quiver of
\underline{\mathcal{F}}
. Then, for any
i\neq j
,
\operatorname{ext}^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{j})=\hom(\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{j})+\operatorname{ext}^{2}(\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{j})-(\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{j})=-(\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{j})>0
so
\overline{Q}
has an arrow from
\mathcal{F}_{i}
to
\mathcal{F}_{j}
. Furthermore, for any
1\leqslant i\leqslant r
,
\operatorname{ext}^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{i})=2-(\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{i})>2
and since
\mathcal{A}
is a
2
-Calabi–Yau category,
\operatorname{ext}^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{F}_{i})
is even. Therefore,
\overline{Q}
has at last
4
loops at each vertex.
∎

7.2.
Representations of the fundamental group of a surface

7.2.1.
The stack of representations of the fundamental group of a surface

Let
\Sigma_{g}
be a closed orientable topological surface without boundary, of genus
g
. We fix a point
p\in\Sigma_{g}
. Let
r,d
be integers with
r>0
. If
d\neq 0
, we write
r=\underline{r}\gcd(r,d)
and
d=\underline{d}\gcd(r,d)
, fix throughout a primitive
\underline{r}
th root of unity
\zeta_{\underline{r}}
, and set
\lambda=\zeta_{\underline{r}}^{\underline{d}}
. If
d=0
we set
\lambda=1
. The fundamental group of
\Sigma_{g}\smallsetminus\{p\}
is

\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g}\smallsetminus\{p\})=\left\langle l,x_{i},y_{i},1\leqslant i\leqslant g\mid l\prod_{i=1}^{g}(x_{i}y_{i}x_{i}^{-1}y_{i}^{-1})=1\right\rangle.

Its group algebra
\mathbf{C}[\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g}\smallsetminus\{p\})]
is the algebra generated by
l,x_{i},y_{i}
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant g
, localised at
x_{i},y_{i}
, with the relation
l\prod_{i=1}^{g}x_{i}y_{i}x_{i}^{-1}y_{i}^{-1}=1
.

We are interested in local systems on
\Sigma_{g}\smallsetminus\{p\}
whose monodromy around
p
is given by the multiplication by
\lambda
and therefore in representations of the algebra
\mathbf{C}[\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g}\smallsetminus\{p\}),\lambda]=\mathbf{C}[\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g}\smallsetminus\{p\})]/\langle l-\lambda\rangle
. Note that the rank of any such local system is divisible by
\underline{r}
if
d\neq 0
.

We denote by

Z^{\Betti}_{g,r,d}\subseteq\mathrm{GL}_{r}^{\times 2g}

the variety cut out by the matrix valued equation
\prod_{i=1}^{g}(A_{i},B_{i})=\lambda\cdot\Id_{r\times r}
. Then set

\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d}\coloneqq\operatorname{Spec}\left(\Gamma(Z^{\Betti}_{g,r,d})^{\mathrm{GL}_{r}}\right).

We define
\mathfrak{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d}
to be the stack of
r
-dimensional representations of
\mathbf{C}[\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g}\smallsetminus\{p\}),\lambda]
. There is an equivalence of stacks
\mathfrak{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d}\simeq Z^{\Betti}_{g,r,d}/\mathrm{GL}_{r}
. In particular,
\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d}
is the affinization of
\mathfrak{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d}
, and the affinization morphism
\mathtt{JH}\colon\mathfrak{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d}
is a good moduli space.

7.2.2.
The dg-category of representations of the fundamental group of a surface

Recall that a topological space
M
is called
acyclic
if its universal cover is contractible.

Theorem 7.2

(
[
14
, Corollary 6.2.4]
)
.

Let
M
be a compact orientable acyclic manifold of dimension
d
without boundary. Then the fundamental group algebra
\mathbf{C}[\pi_{1}(M)]
is a
d
-Calabi–Yau algebra.

As an immediate corollary, we obtain the following.

Corollary 7.3
.

For any
g\geqslant 1
, the group algebra
\mathbf{C}[\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g})]
of the fundamental group of a Riemann surface of genus
g
,

\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g})=\left\langle x_{i},y_{i},1\leqslant i\leqslant g\mid\prod_{i=1}^{g}x_{i}y_{i}x_{i}^{-1}y_{i}^{-1}=1\right\rangle

is a
2
-Calabi–Yau algebra.

Proof.

Such a surface is compact, orientable, and acyclic.
∎

The cohomological Hall algebra of representations of the fundamental group algebra of a Riemann surface was defined and studied in terms of brane tiling algebras in
[
15
]
, where it was shown that it satisfies the cohomological integrality theorem (regardless of genus). It is shown in
[
53
]
that the CoHA defined this way agrees with the more direct construction analogous to the Schiffmann–Vasserot definition of the CoHA of compactly supported sheaves on
\mathbf{A}^{2}
, and thus (adapting the arguments of §
9.2
) to the CoHA defined in this paper in terms of the RHom complex. This Hall algebra appears also in
[
61
]
.

The analogue of Corollary
7.3
is expected to be true for
\mathbf{C}[\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g}\smallsetminus\{p\}),\lambda]
with
g\geqslant 1
, although we have not been able to locate a precise reference. We use instead the fact that these algebras are localizations of multiplicative preprojective algebras, which are known to be
2
-Calabi–Yau algebras (
[
50
]
).

Let
Q=(Q_{0},Q_{1})=S_{g}
be the quiver with one vertex and
g
loops
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{g}
. The doubled quiver
\overline{Q}
has arrows
\alpha_{i},\alpha_{i}^{*}
,
1\leqslant i\leqslant g
. Let
A
be the universal localisation of
\mathbf{C}\overline{Q}
with respect to the elements
1+\alpha_{i}\alpha_{i}^{*}
and
1+\alpha_{i}^{*}\alpha_{i}
for
1\leqslant i\leqslant g
. We let

\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)\coloneqq A\Big/\left\langle\prod_{i=1}^{g}(1+\alpha_{i}\alpha_{i}^{*})(1+\alpha_{i}^{*}\alpha_{i})^{-1}-\lambda\right\rangle

be the corresponding multiplicative preprojective algebra. Since we have an explicit presentation of
\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)
as a quotient of a localised quiver algebra by a set of relations, we may define the derived multiplicative preprojective algebra
\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\lambda}(Q)
as in §
6.2.4
. This is the same as the derived multiplicative preprojective algebra defined in
[
50
]
. The stack of finite-dimensional representations of
\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\lambda}(Q)
satisfies Assumptions
1
-
5
by §
6.2
, and so all that remains is to consider the 2CY property.

We let
\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)^{\prime}
be the universal localisation of
\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)
at
\alpha_{i}
,
1\leqslant i\leqslant g
.

Proposition 7.4

(
[
12
, Proposition 2]
)
.

We have an isomorphism of algebras

\mathbf{C}[\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g}\smallsetminus\{p\}),\lambda]\cong\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)^{\prime}.

Proof.

Crawley-Boevey only states that these two algebras are Morita equivalent, i.e. that the category
\operatorname{Rep}(\mathbf{C}[\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g}\smallsetminus\{p\}),\lambda])
is equivalent to the category
\operatorname{Rep}(\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)^{\prime})
. But the arguments of his proof give an isomorphism of the algebras. The map sends
x_{i}
to
\alpha_{i}
and
y_{i}
to
\alpha_{i}^{-1}+\alpha_{i}^{*}
.
∎

Thanks to this proposition, we can realise the stack of finite-dimensional representations of
\mathbf{C}[\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g}\smallsetminus\{p\}),\lambda]
as an open substack of the stack of finite-dimensional representations of
\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)
. Letting
\mathscr{D}=\operatorname{Perf}_{\dg}(\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)^{\prime})
be the dg-category of perfect
\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)^{\prime}
-modules, we have a fully faithful localisation functor

\mathscr{D}\rightarrow\mathscr{C},

and
\mathscr{D}
is quasi-equivalent to
\operatorname{Perf}_{\dg}(\mathbf{C}[\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g}\smallsetminus\{p\}),\lambda])
. We can therefore rely on the favourable properties of the multiplicative preprojective algebra
\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)
:

Proposition 7.5

(
[
50
, Theorem 1.2+Proposition 4.4]
)
.

\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)
is a (left)
2
-Calabi–Yau algebra, and the natural morphism of dgas
\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\lambda}(Q)\rightarrow\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)
is a quasi-isomorphism.

By
[
4
]
it follows that the full subcategory containing any collection of simple
\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)
-modules carries a right 2CY structure, and so the category of
\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)
-modules satisfies Assumption
6
. Restricting to collections of simple objects arising from
\mathbf{C}[\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g}\smallsetminus\{p\}),\lambda]
-modules via Proposition
7.4
we deduce that the category of
\mathbf{C}[\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g}\smallsetminus\{p\}),\lambda]
-modules also satisfies Assumption
6

7.2.3.
The RHom-complex

We have an explicit projective resolution of the multiplicative preprojective algebra
\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)
as a
\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)
-bimodule and it gives an explicit presentation of the RHom-complex (§
5.1.1
) on the square
\mathfrak{M}_{\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)}
of the stack of finite-dimensional representations of
\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)
. By considering the fully faithful embedding
\mathscr{D}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
, the RHom complex on
\mathfrak{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d}\times\mathfrak{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d}
is the restriction to
\mathfrak{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d}\times\mathfrak{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d}
of the RHom-complex on
\mathfrak{M}_{\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)}
. The latter can be described explicitly thanks to the
3
-term projective resolution of
\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)
as a
\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)
-bimodule,
P_{\bullet}=P_{0}\xrightarrow{\alpha}P_{1}\xrightarrow{\beta}P_{0}
given in
[
50
, Proposition 3.12]
(since
Q=S_{g}
contains a cycle, see
[
50
]
).
It is the same as the three term complex from Corollary
6.22
calculating
\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\lambda}(Q)}(M,N)
.

7.2.4.
The Euler form

By Proposition
7.4
and the discussion following it (§
7.2.2
), we have a fully faithful functor from the derived category of representations of the deformed fundamental group algebra
\mathbf{C}[\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g}\smallsetminus\{p\}),\lambda]
to the derived category of representations of the multiplicative preprojective algebra
\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)
.

We determine the Euler form of the multiplicative preprojective algebra.

Lemma 7.6
.

The Euler form of
\operatorname{Rep}(\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q))
is given by

(7.1)

\begin{split}\mathbf{Z}\times\mathbf{Z}&\longrightarrow\mathbf{Z}\\
(d,e)&\longmapsto 2(1-g)de\end{split}

Proof.

This follows from Corollary
6.22
.
∎

Corollary 7.7
.

The Euler form of the category of finite-dimensional representations of the deformed fundamental group algebra
\mathbf{C}[\pi(\Sigma_{g}\smallsetminus\{p\}),\lambda]
is given by (
7.1
). In particular, if
g\geqslant 2
this category is a totally negative 2CY category.

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of the fully faithful embedding
\mathscr{D}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
which induces a fully faithful embedding of the triangulated categories
\mathcal{D}^{\bdd}(\operatorname{Rep}(\mathbf{C}[\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g}\smallsetminus\{p\}),\lambda]))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}^{\bdd}(\operatorname{Rep}(\Lambda^{\lambda}(Q)))
.
∎

7.3.
Semistable Higgs bundles

7.3.1.
The stack of Higgs sheaves

Let
C
be a complex smooth projective curve. A
Higgs sheaf
on
C
is a pair
(\mathcal{F},\theta)
of a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
C
together with an
\mathcal{O}_{C}
-linear map
\theta\colon\mathcal{F}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}\otimes K_{C}
(the
Higgs field
) where
K_{C}
is the canonical bundle of
C
. The rank
r
and degree
d
of a Higgs sheaf are defined to be the rank and degree of the underlying coherent sheaf, while the slope
\mu(\mathcal{F},\theta)=\mu(\mathcal{F})
is likewise defined to be
d/r
. We let
\mathbf{Z}^{2,+}=\{(r,d)\in\mathbf{Z}^{2}\mid r>0\text{ or }(r=0\text{ and }d\geqslant 0)\}
. We let
\mathbf{Higgs}(C)
be the (Abelian) category of Higgs sheaves on
C
and let
\mathfrak{Higgs}(C)=\bigsqcup_{(r,d)\in\mathbf{Z}^{2,+}}\mathfrak{Higgs}_{(r,d)}(C)
be the stack of Higgs sheaves on
C
.

7.3.2.
Semistable Higgs sheaves

Let
(\mathcal{F},\theta)
be a Higgs sheaf. It is called semistable if for any subsheaf
\mathcal{G}\subseteq\mathcal{F}
such that
\theta(\mathcal{G})\subseteq\mathcal{G}\otimes K_{C}
, we have the inequality of slopes

\mu(\mathcal{G})=\frac{\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{G})}{\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{G})}\leqslant\mu(\mathcal{F})=\frac{\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{F})}{\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{F})}.

For
\theta\in\mathbf{Q}\cup\{\infty\}
, we denote by
\mathbf{Higgs}^{\sst}_{\theta}(C)
the full subcategory of the category of Higgs sheaves, containing those Higgs sheaves that are semistable of slope
\theta
, along with the zero Higgs sheaf. It is an Abelian subcategory of
\mathbf{Higgs}(C)
, the category of all Higgs sheaves. The stack of semistable Higgs sheaves of rank
r
and degree
d
,
\mathfrak{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C)
, is an open substack of
\mathfrak{Higgs}(C)
. It is of finite type and can be realised as a global quotient stack. We spell this out in the next section.

7.3.3.
The BNR correspondence

We may consider the category of Higgs sheaves as a subcategory of the category of coherent sheaves on a quasiprojective surface via the BNR-correspondence
[
8
,
67
]
. Let
S=\Tan^{*}\!C
and
\overline{S}=\mathbf{P}_{C}(\mathcal{O}_{C}\oplus K_{C})
. The projective surface
\overline{S}
is a smooth compactification of
S
. We let
D_{\infty}
be the complement of the open immersion
S\subseteq\overline{S}
. We let
\pi\colon\Tan^{*}\!C\rightarrow C
and
\overline{\pi}\colon\overline{S}\rightarrow C
be the natural projections.

There is an equivalence of categories between the category of coherent sheaves
\mathcal{F}
on
S
for which
\pi_{*}\mathcal{F}=\mathrm{R}^{0}\pi_{*}\mathcal{F}
is coherent and the category of Higgs sheaves on
C
. Therefore, the category of Higgs sheaves on
C
is equivalent to the category of coherent sheaves on
\overline{S}
whose support does not intersect
D_{\infty}
. Furthermore, semistability of the corresponding Higgs bundle is equivalent to Gieseker-semistability for the polarization of
\overline{S}
given by an arbitrary choice of very ample class. Via the BNR correspondence, we consider
\mathfrak{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C)
as an open substack of the stack of semistable coherent sheaves on
\overline{S}
. Likewise, we consider the coarse moduli space
\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C)
as an open subscheme of the coarse moduli scheme of semistable sheaves on
\overline{S}
.

7.3.4.
A dg-enhancement of the category of Higgs sheaves

We let
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\dg}(\Coh(\overline{S}))
be the dg-enhancement of the derived category of coherent sheaves on
\overline{S}
(classically constructed as the dg-quotient of the pretriangulated dg-category of complexes in
\Coh(\overline{S})
by the full pretriangulated dg-subcategory of acyclic complexes). We define
\mathcal{D}_{\dg}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Higgs}(C))
as the full pretriangulated dg-subcategory of
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\dg}(\Coh(S))
of bounded complexes whose cohomology sheaves are coherent after applying
\pi_{*}
.

Proposition 7.8
.

Fix a slope
\theta\in\mathbf{Q}
. The category
\mathbf{Higgs}^{\sst}_{\theta}(C)
, considered as a subcategory of the dg-category
\mathcal{D}_{\dg}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbf{Higgs}(C))
, along with its stack of objects, satisfy Assumptions
1
-
6
from §
5
.

Proof.

This follows, via the BNR correspondence, from the statement for semistable coherent sheaves on
S
, which is established in §
6.1
(using that
K_{S}\cong\mathcal{O}_{S}
for Assumption
6
).
∎

7.3.5.
The Euler form

The Euler form of the category of Higgs sheaves on a smooth projective curve factors through the numerical Grothendieck group of the curve

\begin{split}\K(\mathbf{Higgs}(C))&\longrightarrow\mathbf{Z}^{2}\\
\overline{\mathcal{F}}=[(\mathcal{F},\theta)]&\longmapsto(\operatorname{rank}(\mathcal{F}),\deg(\mathcal{F})).\end{split}

It is given by

(7.2)

(\overline{\mathcal{F}},\overline{\mathcal{G}}):=\sum_{i\in\mathbf{Z}}(-1)^{i}\operatorname{ext}^{i}(\overline{\mathcal{F}},\overline{\mathcal{G}})=2(1-g)\mathrm{rank}(\overline{\mathcal{F}})\mathrm{rank}(\overline{\mathcal{G}}).

Proposition 7.9
.

For any
g\geqslant 2
, the Abelian category
\mathbf{Higgs}^{\sst}_{\theta}(C)
of semistable Higgs bundles of fixed finite slope on a smooth projective curve of genus
\geqslant 2
is totally negative.

Proof.

Immediate from (
7.2
).
∎

7.4.
Semistable sheaves on symplectic surfaces

Let
S
be a smooth projective symplectic surface, i.e.
S
is a K3 or Abelian surface. Let
\mathcal{O}_{S}(1)
be an ample line bundle on
S
.
We explain the usual setup for moduli spaces on such a surface.
We endow
\HO^{*}(S,\mathbf{Z})
with the quadratic form

(v_{0},v_{1},v_{2})(w_{0},w_{1},w_{2})=v_{1}w_{1}-v_{0}w_{2}-v_{2}w_{0}\text{, for $v_{i},w_{i}\in\HO^{2i}(S,\mathbf{Z})$.}

The Mukai vector of a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
is defined to be

v(\mathcal{F})=(\operatorname{rank}(\mathcal{F}),\mathrm{c}_{1}(\mathcal{F}),\mathrm{ch}_{2}(\mathcal{F})+\operatorname{rank}(\mathcal{F}))=\mathrm{ch}(\mathcal{F})\cdot\sqrt{\operatorname{td}(S)}\in\HO^{*}(S,\mathbf{Z}).

For any two coherent sheaves
\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F}\in\Coh(S)
the Euler form is given by
Riemann–Roch

(7.3)

\chi(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F})=-v(\mathcal{E})v(\mathcal{F})=-\mathrm{c}_{1}(\mathcal{E})\mathrm{c}_{1}(\mathcal{F})+2\operatorname{rank}(\mathcal{E})\operatorname{rank}(\mathcal{F})+\operatorname{rank}(\mathcal{E})\mathrm{ch}_{2}(\mathcal{F})+\mathrm{ch}_{2}(\mathcal{E})\operatorname{rank}(\mathcal{F}).

Thus the Mukai vector
v=v(\mathcal{F})
of a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
determines its Hilbert polynomial which we denote by
P_{v}(t)
. As in §
6.1.1
we define Gieseker semistable sheaves on
S
with respect to
\mathcal{O}_{S}(1)
. Let
\mathfrak{Coh}^{\sst}_{v}(S)\subseteq\mathfrak{Coh}^{\sst}_{P_{v}(E)}(S)
be the open and closed substack of Gieseker semistable coherent sheaves on
S
with Mukai vector
v
.

Let
v_{0}\in\HO^{*}(S,\mathbf{Z})
be a primitive Mukai vector and suppose that
\mathcal{O}_{S}(1)
is
v_{0}
-generic (i.e. such that all Gieseker semistable sheaves with Mukai vector
v_{0}
are automatically stable). Let
\Coh^{\sst}(S,v_{0})\subseteq\Coh(S)
be the full Abelian subcategory of pure dimension 1 Gieseker semistable sheaves with Mukai vector a multiple of
v_{0}
.
We call
\Coh^{\sst}(S,v_{0})
the category of
one dimensional semistable sheaves of slope
v_{0}
.
Since we demand sheaves in
\Coh^{\sst}(S,v_{0})
to be pure of dimension one, the category
\Coh^{\sst}(S,v_{0})
can be nonzero only if
v_{0}=(0,\beta,\chi)
for
0\neq\beta\in H^{2}(S)

Proposition 7.10
.

Let
v_{0}\in\HO^{2}(S)
be a Mukai vector for which
\mathcal{O}_{S}(1)
is
v_{0}
-generic. Suppose
v_{0}^{2}>0
, then the category of one dimensional semistable sheaves of slope
v_{0}
is totally negative.

An example of such a Mukai vector
v_{0}
is constructed as follows. Suppose the general smooth curve in the linear system
\lvert\mathcal{O}_{S}(1)\rvert
has genus
g\geqslant 2
. Then the Mukai vector
v_{0}=(0,[C],\chi)
for a smooth curve
C\in\lvert\mathcal{O}_{S}(1)\rvert
and
\chi\in\mathbf{Z}
satisfies
v_{0}^{2}=2g-2>0.

8.
Cohomological Hall algebras for preprojective algebras

8.1.
Notations for quiver representations

Let
Q=(Q_{0},Q_{1})
be a quiver and let
\Pi_{Q}\coloneqq\mathbf{C}\overline{Q}/\langle\rho\rangle
be the associated preprojective algebra.

We establish the notation for the rest of the section, following on from §
2.1
:

(1)

The representation space of
\mathbf{d}
-dimensional representations of the preprojective algebra is
X_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}=\mu^{-1}_{\mathbf{d}}(0)
. We have a closed immersion
i_{\mathbf{d}}\colon X_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}\rightarrow X_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}}
. The stack of
\mathbf{d}
-dimensional representations of the preprojective algebra is denoted
\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}\simeq\mu^{-1}_{\mathbf{d}}(0)/\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{d}}
. The scheme parametrising semisimple
\mathbf{d}
-dimensional representations of
\Pi_{Q}
is denoted
\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}\cong\mu_{\mathbf{d}}^{-1}(0)/\!\!/\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{d}}
. The semisimplification map is
\mathtt{JH}_{\mathbf{d}}\colon\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}
.

(2)

For
\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}
, we fix a
Q_{0}
-graded
\mathbf{d}\coloneqq\mathbf{d}^{(1)}+\mathbf{d}^{(2)}
-dimensional
Q_{0}
-graded complex vector space
\mathbf{C}^{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}+\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
and a
\mathbf{d}^{(1)}
-dimensional
Q_{0}
-graded subspace
\mathbf{C}^{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\subseteq\mathbf{C}^{\mathbf{d}}
.

(3)

We let
P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\subseteq\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{d}}
be the stabiliser of the subspace
\mathbf{C}^{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}
. It is a parabolic subgroup. Its unipotent radical is
U_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
and its Levi quotient is
\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
. Its Lie algebra is denoted by
\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
. The Lie algebra of the Levi subgroup is
\mathfrak{l}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}=\mathfrak{gl}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{gl}_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
. We have a
P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
-equivariant quotient map

l\colon\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{l}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}

where
P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
acts on the target via the quotient morphism
P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
and the conjugation map. We give
\mathfrak{n}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\coloneqq\ker(l)
the induced
P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
-equivariant structure.

(4)

We set

\overline{\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}\coloneqq(X_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times X_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}})/P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}

where
P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
acts on
X_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times X_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
via the quotient
P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
.

(5)

We let
F_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
be the closed subvariety of
X_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}}
consisting of elements preserving the subspace
\mathbf{C}^{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\subseteq\mathbf{C}^{\mathbf{d}}
. It is acted on by
P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
and we set
\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}=F_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}/P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
. This is the stack of short exact sequences of
\overline{Q}
-representations where the subrepresentation has dimension vector
\mathbf{d}^{(1)}
and the quotient
\mathbf{d}^{(2)}
. There is a closed immersion

\overline{p}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\colon F_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow X_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}}.

We also denote by

\overline{p}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\colon\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}+\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}

the induced map between the stacks. We denote by

\overline{q}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\colon F_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow X_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times X_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}

the projection. It induces a vector bundle
\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}
and a vector bundle stack
\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
.

(6)

We let
F_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
be the closed subvariety of
X_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}
of elements preserving the subspace
\mathbf{C}^{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\subseteq\mathbf{C}^{\mathbf{d}}
. It is acted on by
P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
and we let
\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}=F_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}/P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
. This is the stack of short exact sequences of
\Pi_{Q}
-representations where the subrepresentation has dimension vector
\mathbf{d}^{(1)}
and the quotient has dimension vector
\mathbf{d}^{(2)}
. We let

q_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\colon F_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow X_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times X_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}

be the natural projection and denote by
p_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\colon F_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow X_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}
the inclusion. We still denote by
p_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\colon\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}+\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
the map between the stacks. It is proper and representable.

(7)

We set
\overline{F}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}=\overline{q}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}^{-1}(\mu_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}^{-1}(0)\times\mu_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}^{-1}(0))
. We have closed immersions

F_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\xrightarrow{i^{\prime}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}\overline{F}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\xrightarrow{i_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}F_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}

and the projection
q_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}^{\prime}\colon\overline{F}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow X_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times X_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
induced by
\overline{q}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
. The stack
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}=\overline{F}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}/P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
is the stack of short exact sequences of
\overline{Q}
-representations such that the subobject and the quotient are representations of
\Pi_{Q}
, respectively of dimension
\mathbf{d}^{(1)}
and
\mathbf{d}^{(2)}
. The morphism
q^{\prime}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
induces a morphism
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
, still denoted
q^{\prime}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
.

(8)

We let

\overline{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}=(X_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times X_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}})/P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}

where
P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
acts on
X_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times X_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
via the quotient
P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
.

(9)

We still denote by
q_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
the map
\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}
obtained from the map
q_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
of (
6
) after quotient by
P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
.

(10)

We let
\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}=Z_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}/P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
where
Z_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\subseteq X_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times X_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\times\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
is the space of triples
(\rho^{(1)},\rho^{(2)},g)
defined by the condition
\mu_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}(\rho^{(1)})\times\mu_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}(\rho^{(2)})=l(g)
. We have a closed immersion

\overline{i_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times i_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}:=(i_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times i_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\times\{0\})\colon X_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times X_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow Z_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}.

We still denote by

\overline{i_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times i_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}\colon\overline{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}

the morphism obtained after quotient by
P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
. We denote by

\overline{q}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\colon F_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow Z_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}

the morphism taking
\rho\mapsto(q_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}(\rho),\mu_{\mathbf{d}}(\rho))
, and we denote by the same symbol the induced morphism of
P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
-quotients.

(11)

We let
\overline{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}=\overline{Z}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}/P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
where

\overline{Z}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\subseteq F_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\times\mathfrak{p}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}

is the subspace of pairs
(\rho,g)
such that
(\mu_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mu_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}})q_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}(\rho)=l(g)
, with its natural
P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
-equivariant structure. The moment map induces a section
s_{\mu}\colon F_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow\overline{Z}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
, defined by
s_{\mu}(x)=(x,\mu_{\mathbf{d}}(x))
. By taking the quotient by
P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
, we obtain a morphism

r\colon\overline{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}

with a section still denoted
s_{\mu}
.

We let
\mathfrak{F}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}=(\overline{F}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\times\mathfrak{n}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}})/P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
. The morphism
r
restricts to the projection of the total space of the vector bundle

\mathfrak{F}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}

with a section denoted by
s^{\prime}_{\mu}
. Note that
\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
is precisely the vanishing locus of this section.

(12)

We let
\widetilde{q}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\colon\overline{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
be the map induced by the group homomorphism
P_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
. It is smooth of dimension
-\mathbf{d}^{(1)}\cdot\mathbf{d}^{(2)}=-\sum_{i\in Q_{0}}(\mathbf{d}^{(1)})_{i}(\mathbf{d}^{(2)})_{i}
.

8.2.
The relative cohomological Hall algebra product for a preprojective algebra

In this section, we recall how the relative cohomological Hall algebra product is defined for the stack of representations of the preprojective algebra of a quiver, following
[
72
,
81
,
19
]
.

Let
\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\Pi_{Q}}\coloneqq(\mathtt{JH}_{\Pi_{Q}})_{*}\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}}^{\mathrm{vir}}
. The relative cohomological Hall algebra is an algebra structure on the object
\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\Pi_{Q}}
of
\mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}))
, i.e. a map

m\colon\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\Pi_{Q}}\boxdot\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\Pi_{Q}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\Pi_{Q}}

as in Definition
3.1
and §
3.2.1
.

8.2.1.
Construction at the sheaf level

Let
\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}
and
\mathbf{d}=\mathbf{d}^{(1)}+\mathbf{d}^{(2)}
. Consider the following commutative diagram.

(8.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{q}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{i_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times i_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathbf{d}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{p}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{q}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

where both the squares are Cartesian. The vertical arrows are closed immersions. In the sequel, we drop the indices
\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}
for horizontal maps since the dimension vectors
\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}
are fixed.

The map
p=p_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
is proper and representable so that we have a morphism of complexes

(8.2)

\alpha_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\colon p_{*}\mathbb{D}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{D}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}}

obtained by dualizing the canonical adjunction map
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}}\rightarrow p_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}
.

Since
\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
and
\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
are smooth, the map
\overline{q}^{\prime}=\overline{q}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
is l.c.i., and therefore there exists a refined pullback by
q=q_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
which we recall at the level of constructible complexes (see also §
4.2
). The map
\overline{q}^{\prime}=\overline{q}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
induces the adjunction map

(8.3)

\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}\rightarrow\overline{q}^{\prime}_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}

which dualizes to

(8.4)

\overline{q}^{\prime}_{!}\mathbb{D}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{D}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}.

Since for a smooth stack
\mathfrak{X}
, we have
\mathbb{D}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{X}}=\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{X}}[2\dim\mathfrak{X}]
, we can rewrite (
8.4
) as

(8.5)

\overline{q}^{\prime}_{!}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}[2\dim\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}]\rightarrow\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}[2\dim\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}].

Applying
(\overline{i_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times i_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}})^{*}
to (
8.5
) together with the base change isomorphism
(\overline{i_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times i_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}})^{*}\overline{q}^{\prime}_{!}\cong q_{!}i_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}^{*}
, we get

(8.6)

q_{!}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}[2\dim\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}]\rightarrow\mathbf{Q}_{\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}[2\dim\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}].

Dualizing (
8.6
), we get the morphism

(8.7)

(\mathbb{D}\mathbf{Q}_{\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}})[-2\dim\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}]\rightarrow q_{*}(\mathbb{D}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}})[-2\dim\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}]

which is the virtual pullback by
q
at the sheaf level.

The map
\widetilde{q}
is smooth so that denoting by
\dim\widetilde{q}
its relative dimension, we may identify
(\widetilde{q})^{!}=(\widetilde{q})^{*}[2\dim\widetilde{q}]
. Using this identity, the inverse of the isomorphism
(\widetilde{q})^{*}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}\rightarrow\mathbf{Q}_{\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}
can be rewritten

(8.8)

\mathbf{Q}_{\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}\rightarrow(\widetilde{q})^{!}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}[-2\dim\widetilde{q}].

By the adjunction
((\widetilde{q})_{!},(\widetilde{q})^{!})
, we get a map

(8.9)

(\widetilde{q})_{!}\mathbf{Q}_{\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}\rightarrow\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}[-2\dim\widetilde{q}].

whose Verdier dual is the pullback by
\widetilde{q}
:

(8.10)

(\mathbb{D}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}})[2\dim\widetilde{q}]\rightarrow(\widetilde{q})_{*}\mathbb{D}\mathbf{Q}_{\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}.

By composing (
8.10
) shifted by
2(\dim\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q}\,\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}-\dim\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}})
with
(\widetilde{q})_{*}
applied to (
8.7
) shifted by
2\dim\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
, we get the morphism

(8.11)

v_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}\colon\mathbb{D}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}[2(\dim\widetilde{q}+\dim\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}-\dim\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}})]\rightarrow(\widetilde{q})_{*}q_{*}\mathbb{D}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}

which is the virtual pullback by
\widetilde{q}\circ q
.

Lemma 8.1
.

We have the equalities

\displaystyle\dim\widetilde{q}+\dim\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}-\dim\mathfrak{Z}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}

\displaystyle=-\langle\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}\rangle_{Q}-\langle\mathbf{d}^{(2)},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}\rangle_{Q}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{d})_{\Pi_{Q}}+\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(1)})_{\Pi_{Q}}+\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{d}^{(2)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)})_{\Pi_{Q}}.

Proof.

This is a straightforward calculation.
∎

Recall that we define
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}}^{\mathrm{vir}}\coloneqq\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}}[-(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{d})_{\Pi_{Q}}]
. We now introduce the coarse moduli space of the preprojective algebra with the first row of the diagram (
8.1
) as in the following commutative diagram:

(8.12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q}\mathbf{d}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathtt{JH}_{\mathbf{d}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathtt{JH}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathtt{JH}_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}

By composing
\oplus_{*}(\mathtt{JH}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathtt{JH}_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}})_{*}
applied to (
8.11
) shifted by
(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{d})_{\Pi_{Q}}
with
\mathtt{JH}_{*}
applied to
(
8.2
)
shifted by
(\mathbf{d},\mathbf{d})_{\Pi_{Q}}
and using that (
8.12
) commutes, we get the map

m_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\colon\oplus_{*}(\mathtt{JH}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathtt{JH}_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}})_{*}\mathbb{D}\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}}\rightarrow(\mathtt{JH}_{\mathbf{d}})_{*}\mathbb{D}\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}}

which is the
(\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)})
-graded piece of the relative CoHA product.

8.2.2.
Upgrade to mixed Hodge modules

Since each
\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}
is a global quotient stack, we can upgrade the morphisms
m_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
in the standard way recalled in §
3.2.1
. The pullback of (
8.11
) along a smooth morphism
j\colon U_{N}\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
admits a canonical upgrade to the category of mixed Hodge modules on
U_{N}
, which we then apply
\oplus_{*}(\mathtt{JH}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathtt{JH}_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}})_{*}j_{*}
to. Picking
U_{N}
the schemes appearing in an acyclic cover of
\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
enables us to upgrade the morphisms
{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\!\tau^{\leqslant n}}v_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}
to morphisms of mixed Hodge module complexes, yielding a morphism
\underline{v}_{\mathbf{d}^{\prime},\mathbf{d}^{\prime\prime}}
in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{+}(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}})
. We upgrade
\mathtt{JH}_{*}\alpha_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
the same way, and composing the resulting morphisms, we define the Hall algebra multiplication on
\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\Pi_{Q}}
. We generalise this Hall algebra, defined in the category of complexes of mixed Hodge modules, to arbitrary categories satisfying Assumptions
1
to
3
in the next section.

8.3.
A hierarchy of cohomological Hall algebras

It is often profitable to consider the Hall algebra on the Borel–Moore homology of the stack of representations in a Serre subcategory of the category of representations for a given algebra. This turns out to be especially true for preprojective algebras, where we will prove our main theorem by considering three different Serre subcategories.

8.3.1.
The full cohomological Hall algebra

We obtain the
full cohomological Hall algebra
by taking derived global sections.

\HO^{*}\!\!\!\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\Pi_{Q}}\coloneqq\HO^{*}\!(\mathtt{JH}_{*}\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}}}^{\mathrm{vir}})\cong\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}\!(\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q}},\underline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathrm{vir}}).

In this case the Serre subcategory of the category of finite-dimensional
\Pi_{Q}
-representations is the entire category. This special case is the subject of Corollary
1.3
.

8.3.2.
The cohomological Hall algebra of the strictly seminilpotent locus

Let
\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{SSN}}_{\Pi_{Q}}
be the closed subvariety of
\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}
parametrise semisimple representations of
\Pi_{Q}
such that the only arrows acting possibly non-trivially are the loops
\alpha\in Q_{1}\subseteq\overline{Q}_{1}=Q_{1}\sqcup Q_{1}^{*}
.

Then, we have a pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{M}^{\mathcal{SSN}}_{\Pi_{Q}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}^{\mathcal{SSN}}_{\Pi_{Q}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathtt{JH}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathcal{SSN}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathtt{JH}_{\Pi_{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

where
\mathfrak{M}^{\mathcal{SSN}}_{\Pi_{Q}}
parametrises strongly seminilpotent representations of
\Pi_{Q}
. It is called the
strongly seminilpotent stack
and its
\mathbf{C}
-points parametrise the seminilpotent representations of
\Pi_{Q}
. These are representations
M
of
\Pi_{Q}
such that there exists a flag
0=M_{0}\subseteq M_{1}\subseteq\ldots\subseteq M_{r}=M
with
M_{i}/M_{i-1}
supported at a single vertex for
1\leqslant i\leqslant r
,
x_{\alpha}M_{i}\subseteq M_{i}
and
x_{\alpha^{*}}M_{i}\subseteq M_{i-1}
for
\alpha\in Q_{1}
.

The algebra structure
i^{!}m
obtained on
\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathcal{SSN}}:=(\mathtt{JH}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathcal{SSN}})_{*}\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathcal{SSN}}}^{\mathrm{vir}}=i^{!}(\mathtt{JH}_{\Pi_{Q}})_{*}\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}}^{\mathrm{vir}}
is the relative strongly seminilpotent Hall algebra. The strongly seminilpotent Hall algebra
\HO^{*}\!\!\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathcal{SSN}}
, is obtained by applying the derived global sections functor to the algebra object
\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathcal{SSN}}
.

This algebra plays a crucial role in our proofs, because it is precisely the (cohomological degree zero part of this) algebra that is described (via
[
7
]
and then
[
32
]
for the translation into cohomology) as a generalised Kac–Moody Lie algebra.

8.3.3.
The cohomological Hall algebra of the nilpotent locus

Let
\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathrm{nil}}
be the closed subvariety of
\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}
parametrising semisimple representations of
\Pi_{Q}
such that all arrows act via the zero morphism. I.e. for each dimension vector
\mathbf{d}
the inclusion
\iota_{\mathbf{d}}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathrm{nil}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}
is a single point, corresponding to the nilpotent module
S_{\mathbf{d}}
. Then we define the
fully nilpotent CoHA
of
\Pi_{Q}

\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathrm{nil}}\coloneqq\HO^{*}(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\mathrm{nil}},\iota^{!}\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\Pi_{Q}}).

This algebra also plays a key role in our proofs; although
\{S_{i}\in\Pi_{Q}\lmod\mid i\in Q_{0}\}
is a specific
\Sigma
-collection, this CoHA models the CoHA of an
arbitrary

\Sigma
-collection: see Corollary
9.10
for the precise statement. This is as close as we come in this paper to upgrading the analytic neighbourhood theorem (Theorem
5.3
) to a statement incorporating Hall algebra products, and is as close as we need to come in order to prove our main theorems.

9.
Cohomological Hall algebras of 2-dimensional categories

9.1.
The CoHA product

In this section, we define the CoHA product, generalising the one given in
[
51
]
. We let
\mathscr{C}
,
\mathcal{A}
,
\varpi\colon\mathfrak{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
be as in §
5.1.1
. Then we define as in the introduction

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{\varpi}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigoplus_{\mathcal{M}_{a}\in\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M})}\varpi_{*}\mathbb{D}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A},a}}[(a,a)_{\mathscr{C}}]

\displaystyle\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\varpi}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\bigoplus_{\mathcal{M}_{a}\in\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M})}\varpi_{\ast}\mathbb{D}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A},a}}\otimes\mathbf{L}^{-(a,a)_{\mathscr{C}}/2}.

We assume that the assumptions stated in §
5.2
, which we briefly recall, are satisfied. Firstly, we require that the forgetful morphism
p\colon\mathfrak{Exact}\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}
from the stack of short exact sequences of objects parametrise by points of
\mathfrak{M}
, to
\mathfrak{M}
, which remembers only the middle term in the sequence, is representable and proper (Assumption
1
). Secondly, we assume that Diagram (
5.2
) is the classical truncation of a diagram in a certain category of globally presented derived stacks over
\mathfrak{M}^{\times 3}
: see Assumptions
2
and
3
and §
4.3
for details.

Pick
a,b\in\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M})
and let
a+b\in\pi_{0}(\mathcal{M})
denote the connected component containing
\pi_{0}(\oplus)(a,b)
. By Assumption
2
we can present the morphism
q\colon\mathfrak{Exact}_{b,a}\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}_{a}\times\mathfrak{M}_{b}
as the truncation of a globally presented quasi-smooth morphism

\operatorname{Tot}_{\mathfrak{M}_{a}\times\mathfrak{M}_{b}}^{Q}(\mathcal{C}_{a,b}^{\bullet})\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}_{a}\times\mathfrak{M}_{b}.

Recall that
\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{a,b}
is quasi-isomorphic to the RHom complex shifted by one on
\mathfrak{M}_{a}\times\mathfrak{M}_{b}
for
a,b\in\pi_{0}(\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{A}})
. As such we have the equality

(a,b)_{\mathscr{C}}=-\vrank(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{a,b}).

We recall that as part of our starting data (§
5.1.2
) we have the commutative diagram

(9.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{M}_{a}\times\mathfrak{M}_{b}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{t_{0}(\operatorname{Tot}^{Q}_{\mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{M}}(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{a,b}))\simeq\mathfrak{Exact}_{b,a}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{M}_{a+b}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{a}\times\mathcal{M}_{b}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}_{a+b}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varpi_{a}\times\varpi_{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varpi_{a+b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{a,b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{a,b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus}

We define the morphism

(9.2)

(\varpi_{a}\times\varpi_{b})_{*}v_{\mathcal{C}_{a,b}}\colon(\varpi_{a}\times\varpi_{b})_{*}\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{a}\times\mathfrak{M}_{b}}\rightarrow(\varpi_{a}\times\varpi_{b})_{*}(q_{a,b})_{*}\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathfrak{Exact}_{b,a}}\otimes\mathbf{L}^{\vrank(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{a,b})}

as the virtual pullback along
q_{a,b}
defined in §
4.4
. Using Assumption
1
(properness of
p
) we define the morphism

(\varpi_{a+b})_{*}\beta\colon(\varpi_{a+b})_{*}(p_{a,b})_{*}\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathfrak{Exact}_{b,a}}\rightarrow(\varpi_{a+b})_{*}\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{a+b}}

as the direct image of the pullback
2
2

2

Recall that we pull back to a scheme smoothly approximating the stack so that this morphism has a lift to MHM complexes.
to an acyclic cover of the Verdier dual of the adjunction
\underline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{a+b}}\rightarrow(p_{a,b})_{*}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathfrak{Exact}_{b,a}}
.
We define the relative CoHA multiplication on
\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\varpi}
, restricted to
\mathcal{M}_{a}\times\mathcal{M}_{b}
, to be given by

\displaystyle m_{a,b}=

\displaystyle((\varpi_{a+b})_{*}\beta\otimes\mathbf{L}^{(-(a+b,a+b)_{\mathscr{C}}/2})\circ(\oplus_{*}(\varpi_{a}\times\varpi_{b})_{*}v_{\mathcal{C}_{a,b}}\otimes\mathbf{L}^{(-(a,a)_{\mathscr{C}}-(b,b)_{\mathscr{C}})/2}).

9.1.1.
Associativity

Proposition 9.1
.

The above algebra structure on
\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\varpi}
is associative.

Proof.

The proof of associativity follows by a standard argument, since we have Assumption
3
in place. We repeat it for convenience, and so that the reader can see what role the material in §
4
plays in ensuring associativity. We consider the MHM version: the version at the level of constructible complexes is proved in exactly the same way.

We consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Filt}_{c,b,a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{Exact}_{b+c,a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}_{a+b+c}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}_{a}\times\mathfrak{Exact}_{c,b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}_{a}\times\mathfrak{M}_{b+c}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}_{a}\times\mathfrak{M}_{b}\times\mathfrak{M}_{c}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{a,b+c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}\times p_{a,b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{a,b,c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{a,b,c}}

By Assumption
2

q_{a,b+c}
is the truncation of a globally presented quasi-smooth morphism, for which the pullback is isomorphic to
\beta
, by Assumption
3
. So we can write
\mathfrak{Filt}_{c,b,a}=t_{0}(\operatorname{Tot}_{\mathfrak{M}_{a}\times\mathfrak{M}_{b}\times\mathfrak{M}_{c}}^{Q}(\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}))
for some perfect complex
\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}
. Combining Propositions
4.9
and
4.10
we deduce that the composition

m_{a,b+c}\circ(\operatorname{id}\boxdot m_{b,c})\colon\oplus_{*}\varpi^{\times 3}_{*}\left(\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{a}}\boxdot\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{b}}\boxdot\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{c}}\right)\rightarrow\varpi_{*}\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{a+b+c}}

is given by the composition of the virtual pullback along
\delta\beta
in Diagram (
5.2
):

\oplus_{*}\varpi^{\times 3}_{*}v_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\oplus_{*}\varpi^{\times 3}_{*}\left(\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{a}}\boxdot\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{b}}\boxdot\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{c}}\right)\rightarrow(q_{a,b,c})_{*}\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{Filt}_{c,b,a}}\otimes\mathbf{L}^{s/2}

and

\varpi_{*}\!\left(p_{*}\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathfrak{Filt}_{c,b,a}}\otimes\mathbf{L}^{s/2}\rightarrow\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{a+b+c}}\right).

Here,

\displaystyle s/2=

\displaystyle-(a,a)/2-(b,b)/2-(c,c)/2+\vrank(\mathcal{E}^{\bullet})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(a,a)/2-(b,b)/2-(c,c)/2-(a,b)-(a,c)-(b,c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(a+b+c,a+b+c)/2.

By Assumption
3
and Proposition
4.11
, the virtual pullback
v_{\mathcal{E}}
is equal to the virtual pullback along
\gamma\alpha
, and the result follows.
∎

9.1.2.
CoHA associated to a submonoid

We assume, as in §
3.3
that we are given an inclusion of monoids
\imath\colon(\mathcal{N},\oplus)\rightarrow(\mathcal{M},\oplus)
such that the diagram (
3.1
) is Cartesian. Then by Lemma
3.4
, the constructible complex
\imath^{!}\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\varpi}
inherits an algebra structure from the algebra structure on
\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\varpi}
. Precisely, we define the morphism

\imath^{!}\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\varpi}\boxdot\imath^{!}\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\varpi}\rightarrow\imath^{!}\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\varpi}

as the composition of the natural isomorphism

\imath^{!}\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\varpi}\boxdot\imath^{!}\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\varpi}\cong\imath^{!}(\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\varpi}\boxdot\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\varpi})

and
\iota^{!}m
.

9.2.
Comparison of the products for preprojective CoHAs

If
\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q}}
is the moduli stack of representations of the preprojective algebra of a quiver, the constructions of §
8.2
and §
9.1
provide us with two a priori different products on
\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\varpi}=\varpi_{*}\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}}
. In this section, we show that these two products coincide.

9.2.1.
Total space of the RHom complex for the doubled quiver

We give an explicit presentation of the RHom complex on
\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
for
\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}
.

Let
V_{\mathbf{d}^{(j)}}
,
j=1,2
be trivial
\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}
-graded vector bundles on
X_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(j)}}
of rank
\mathbf{d}^{(j)}
. We have a
2
-term complex of vector bundles on
X_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times X_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
, situated in cohomological degrees
-1
and
0
:

\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{\overline{Q}}=\left(\bigoplus_{i\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{C}}((V_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}})_{i},(V_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}})_{i})\xrightarrow{d}\bigoplus_{i\xrightarrow{\alpha}j\in\overline{Q}_{1}}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{C}}((V_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}})_{i},(V_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}})_{j})\right).

Let
x=(x_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\overline{Q}_{1}}\in X_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}
and
y=(y_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in\overline{Q}_{1}}\in X_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
. For
z\in\bigoplus_{i\in Q_{0}}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{C}}((V_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}})_{i},(V_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}})_{i})
, we have

d_{(x,y)}z=(z_{j}y_{\alpha}-x_{\alpha}z_{i})_{i\xrightarrow{\alpha}j\in\overline{Q}_{1}}.

We have a natural
\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathrm{GL}_{\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
-equivariant structure on
\mathcal{C}_{\overline{Q}}
so that we can consider
\mathcal{C}_{\overline{Q}}
as a
2
-term complex on
\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
. The following is easy and well-known (cf. §
6
).

Proposition 9.2
.

The
2
-term complex
\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{\overline{Q}}
on
\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
is quasi-isomorphic to the RHom complex shifted by one.

Corollary 9.3
.

The total space of
\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{\overline{Q}}
is isomorphic as a vector bundle stack over
\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
to the map

\pi_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\colon\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\overline{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}

given in (
5
).

9.2.2.
Total space of the RHom complex for preprojective algebras

Recall the explicit presentation of the RHom complex for preprojective algebras given in (
2.6
). From the definitions of the complexes
\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{\Pi_{Q}}
and
\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{\overline{Q}}
, the following three lemmas are immediate.

Lemma 9.4
.

The restriction of
\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{\overline{Q}}
to
\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
is equal to
\mathcal{C}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\leqslant 0}
.

Lemma 9.5
.

The total space
\operatorname{Tot}(\mathcal{C}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\leqslant 0})
is isomorphic as a vector bundle stack over
\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
to the map
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)}}\times\mathfrak{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
defined in (
7
).

Lemma 9.6
.

The vector bundle
V=\pi_{\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}^{*}\mathcal{C}^{1}
on
\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}^{(1)},\mathbf{d}^{(2)}}
is isomorphic to the vector bundle
