Comparison with the “3d” definition of BPS sheaves and BPS cohomology for Higgs bundles

Let
C
be a smooth projective connected curve of genus
g\geqslant 2
. In
[
46
]
Kinjo–Koseki give a definition of the BPS sheaf
\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\theta}^{\Dol,\mathrm{3d}}
and BPS cohomology
\aBPS_{\theta}^{\Dol,\mathrm{3d}}
for the categories
\mathbf{Higgs}_{\theta}^{\sst}(C)
.

We recall their definition of
\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\theta}^{\Dol,\mathrm{3d}}(C)
.
In
[
47
, Theorem 5.6]
the moduli stack
\mathfrak{M}_{X,\theta}
of 1-dimensional semistable sheaves on
X\coloneqq\operatorname{Tot}_{C}(K_{C}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{C})
of slope
\theta
is realized as a critical locus of a function
f\colon\mathfrak{M}^{\sst}_{\operatorname{Tot}_{C}(K_{C}(p)),\theta}\to\mathbf{C}
on the moduli stack of one-dimensional semistable sheaves on
\operatorname{Tot}_{C}(K_{C}(p))
of the same slope
\theta
, where
p\in C
is a closed point.
The BPS sheaf for 1-dimensional semistable sheaves on
X
is defined just as in the case of symmetric quivers with potential. Let
\mathfrak{M}_{X,\theta}^{\sst}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}_{X,\theta}
be the open substack of semistable sheaves of slope
\theta
.
Consider the vanishing cycle sheaf
\phi_{f}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\sst}_{X}}
and the good moduli space
\widetilde{\mathtt{JH}}\colon\mathfrak{M}^{\sst}_{X}\to\mathcal{M}^{\sst}_{X}
. Define the BPS sheaf by
\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{X}[-1]\coloneqq\tau^{\leqslant 1}\widetilde{\mathtt{JH}}_{*}\phi_{f}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\sst}_{X}}
. By pushing forward along the projection
\mathcal{M}^{\sst}_{X}\to\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{Tot}_{C}(K_{C}),\theta}^{\sst}=\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{\theta}(C)
we give the “3d” definition of the BPS sheaf

\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\theta}^{\Dol,\mathrm{3d}}\coloneqq(\mathcal{M}^{\sst}_{X}\to\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{\theta}(C))_{\ast}\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{X}
, which is a semisimple mixed Hodge module by
[
46
, Proposition 5.12]
.

Let
\underline{\mathcal{IC}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}^{\Dol}(C))\coloneqq\bigoplus_{d/r=\theta}\underline{\mathcal{IC}}(\mathcal{M}_{d,r}^{\Dol}(C))
. Since
\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\theta}^{\Dol,\mathrm{3d}}
and
\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot-\mathrm{Lie}}(\underline{\mathcal{IC}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}^{\Dol}(C))
are semisimple mixed Hodge modules, the following computation in
\K(\mathcal{D}^{+}(\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}^{\Dol}(C))))
implies that they represent the same class in
\K(\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}^{\Dol}(C)))
.

\displaystyle[\Sym_{\boxdot}(\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\theta}^{\Dol,\mathrm{3d}}(C)\otimes\HO_{\mathbf{C}^{\ast}})]

\displaystyle=[\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\theta}^{\Dol}(C)]

\displaystyle(\text{\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{Number}{kinjo2021cohomological}{}{}, Theorem~5.16]}})

\displaystyle=\left[\Sym_{\boxdot}\left(\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot-\mathrm{Lie}}\left(\underline{\mathcal{IC}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}^{\Dol}(C))\right)\otimes\HO_{\mathbf{C}^{\ast}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle(\text{Theorem~\ref{theorem:pbwtotnegative2CY}}).

This implies that there is some non-canonical isomorphism

(13.3)

\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\theta}^{\Dol,\mathrm{3d}}(C)\cong\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot-\mathrm{Lie}}(\underline{\mathcal{IC}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}^{\Dol}(C)))

Unlike for the case of quivers, the 3d-definition of the BPS sheaf
\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\theta}^{\Dol,\text{3d}}(C)\subseteq\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\theta}^{\Dol}(C)
and BPS cohomology
\aBPS_{\theta}^{\Dol,\text{3d}}(C)\subseteq\HO^{*}(\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\theta}^{\Dol}(C))
have not been shown to be closed under the commutator bracket (although one could try to pursue a similar line of reasoning as in
[
25
]
) and therefore are not obviously Lie algebra objects. Consequently, we cannot mimic §
13.1
to obtain a canonical morphism
\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot-\mathrm{Lie}}(\underline{\mathcal{IC}}(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}^{\Dol}(C)))\to\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{\theta}^{\Dol,\mathrm{3d}}(C)
.
Here we see the advantage of defining the BPS sheaf and BPS cohomology as we do in this paper: they automatically have the structure of a Lie algebra object.

14.
Nonabelian Hodge theory for stacks

Let
C
be a smooth projective complex curve of genus
g
. For
\theta\in\mathbf{Q}\cup\{\infty\}
we recall from §
7.3
the definition of the category
\mathbf{Higgs}^{\sst}_{\theta}(C)
: the full subcategory of the category of Higgs sheaves on
C
that are either semistable of slope
\theta
, or the zero Higgs sheaf. This category is Abelian, finite length, and closed under extensions in
\mathbf{Higgs}(C)
.

By the classical nonabelian Hodge isomorphism
[
33
,
26
,
68
]
, there is a homeomorphism

\Psi_{r,d}\colon\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C)\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d}.

This homeomorphism induces an isomorphism in Borel–Moore homology

(14.1)

\Phi\colon\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C),\mathbf{Q})\rightarrow\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}(\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d},\mathbf{Q}).

One of our main motivations was to produce an isomorphism in Borel–Moore homology for moduli
stacks
, analogous to (
14.1
). Note that if the genus of
C
is zero or one, such an isomorphism is known to exist, since the Borel–Moore homology of the Dolbeault and Betti stacks can be explicitly calculated, see
[
20
]
for details. So in this paper we concentrate on the case
g\geqslant 2
, i.e. the case in which
\mathbf{Higgs}^{\sst}_{\theta}(C)
is totally negative.

For a nonzero rational number
\theta
, which we may write as
\theta=a/b
with
a,b\in\mathbf{Z}
,
a>0
and
\gcd(a,b)=1
, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{\theta}(C)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\coprod_{n\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{na,nb}(C)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,\theta}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\coprod_{n\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geqslant 0}}\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,na,nb}.

We define
\mathfrak{M}^{\Dol}_{\theta}(C)
and
\mathfrak{M}^{\Betti}_{g,\theta}
similarly. We define

(14.2)

\displaystyle\aBPS^{\Betti}_{\mathrm{Lie},g,\theta}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\operatorname{Free}_{\mathrm{Lie}}\left(\bigoplus_{n\geqslant 0}\ICA(\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,na,nb})\right)

\displaystyle\aBPS^{\Dol}_{\mathrm{Lie},\theta}(C)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\operatorname{Free}_{\mathrm{Lie}}\left(\bigoplus_{n\geqslant 0}\ICA(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{na,nb}(C))\right).

The Lie algebra
\aBPS^{\Dol}_{\mathrm{Lie},\theta}(C)
carries a bigrading (taking into account ranks and degrees), and for
r\geqslant 1
and
d\in\mathbf{Z}
we define
\aBPS^{\Dol}_{\mathrm{Lie},r,d}
to be the piece of
\aBPS^{\Dol}_{\mathrm{Lie},\theta}(C)
of bidegree
(r,d)
, where
\theta=d/r
. We define
\aBPS^{\Betti}_{\mathrm{Lie},g,r,d}
similarly.

Lemma 14.1
.

Let
\theta\in\mathbf{Q}
. The morphism
\Psi\colon\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{\theta}(C)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,\theta}
is an isomorphism of monoid objects in the category of topological spaces.

Proof.

Since we are working in the category of topological spaces it is sufficient to show that the two morphisms

\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{n^{\prime}a,n^{\prime}b}(C)\times\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{n^{\prime\prime}a,n^{\prime\prime}b}(C)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,(n^{\prime}+n^{\prime\prime})a,(n^{\prime}+n^{\prime\prime})b}

given by
\Psi_{(n^{\prime}+n^{\prime\prime})a,(n^{\prime}+n^{\prime\prime})b}\circ\oplus
and
\oplus\circ(\Psi_{n^{\prime}a,n^{\prime}b}\times\Psi_{n^{\prime\prime}a,n^{\prime\prime}b})
are the same at the level of points. This follows from the construction of
\Psi
: given a polystable Higgs bundle
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{F}_{l}
the corresponding semisimple twisted
\pi_{1}(C)
-module is explicitly constructed via the nonabelian Hodge isomorphism applied to the summands
\mathcal{F}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{F}_{l}
, see
[
68
]
for details.
∎

We denote by

\displaystyle p_{\Dol}\colon

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}^{\Dol}_{\theta}(C)\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{\theta}(C)

\displaystyle p_{\Betti}\colon

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}^{\Betti}_{g,\theta}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,\theta}

the morphisms from the moduli stacks to the moduli spaces of objects, and define
\underline{\mathscr{A}}^{\Dol}_{\theta}(C)\coloneqq p_{\Dol,*}\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\Dol}_{\theta}(C)}^{\mathrm{vir}}
and
\underline{\mathscr{A}}^{\Betti}_{g,\theta}\coloneqq p_{\Betti,*}\mathbb{D}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\Betti}_{g,\theta}}^{\mathrm{vir}}
. For the next theorems we assume that the genus of
C
is at least
2
so that the relevant categories of Higgs bundles and twisted representations of the fundamental group are totally negative. The following is a special case of (the MHM version of) Theorem
1.2
.

Theorem 14.2
.

Assume
g(C)\geqslant 2
. There are isomorphisms of algebra objects in
\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{\theta}(C))
and
\mathrm{MHM}(\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,\theta})
respectively:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\mathrm{-Alg}}\left(\underline{\mathcal{IC}}(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{\theta}(C))\right)\rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{0}(\underline{\mathscr{A}}^{\Dol}_{\theta}(C))

\displaystyle\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\mathrm{-Alg}}\left(\underline{\mathcal{IC}}(\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{\theta})\right)\rightarrow

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{0}(\underline{\mathscr{A}}^{\Betti}_{g,\theta})

extending the inclusions
\underline{\mathcal{IC}}(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{\theta}(C))\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{0}(\underline{\mathscr{A}}^{\Dol}_{\theta}(C))
and
\underline{\mathcal{IC}}(\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,\theta}))\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{0}(\underline{\mathscr{A}}^{\Betti}_{g,\theta})
from §
10.2
.

The following is a special case of Theorem
1.5
.

Theorem 14.3
.

Assume
g(C)\geqslant 2
. The natural morphisms of mixed Hodge structures

\displaystyle\Sym_{\boxdot}\left(\operatorname{Free}_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\ICA(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{\theta}(C)))\otimes\HO_{\mathbf{C}^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\HO^{*}(\underline{\mathscr{A}}^{\Dol}_{\theta}(C))

\displaystyle\Sym_{\boxdot}\left(\operatorname{Free}_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\ICA(\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,\theta}))\otimes\HO_{\mathbf{C}^{\ast}}\right)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\HO^{*}(\underline{\mathscr{A}}^{\Betti}_{g,\theta})

are isomorphisms.

The following theorem is proved for
g(C)\leqslant 1
in
[
20
]
. It is our version of the nonabelian Hodge isomorphism for stacks:

Theorem 14.4
.

There is a natural isomorphism in
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{c}}^{+}(\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d})

(14.3)

\Psi_{*}p_{\Dol,*}\mathbb{D}\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C)}\cong p_{\Betti,*}\mathbb{D}\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d}}.

Taking derived global sections, we deduce that there is a natural isomorphism in Borel–Moore homology between the Dolbeault and the Betti stacks:

(14.4)

\Phi\colon\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}(\mathfrak{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C),\mathbf{Q})\cong\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}(\mathfrak{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d},\mathbf{Q}).

Proof.

The intersection complex is a topological invariant
[
28
]
, so since
\Psi_{r,d}
is a diffeomorphism there is a canonical isomorphism

\Psi_{r,d,*}\mathcal{IC}(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C))\cong\mathcal{IC}(\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d}).

Fix
\theta=r/d
. By Lemma
14.1
there is an isomorphism

(14.5)

\Sym_{\boxdot}\left(\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\mathrm{-Lie}}(\Psi_{*}\mathcal{IC}(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{\theta}(C)))\otimes\HO_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}\right)\cong\Psi_{*}\!\left(\Sym_{\boxdot}\left(\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot\mathrm{-Lie}}(\mathcal{IC}(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{\theta}(C))\otimes\HO_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}\right)\right).

Combining (
14.5
) with the two isomorphisms of Theorem
14.3
yields the isomorphism (
14.3
). Then restricting to
\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d}
and taking derived global sections yields the isomorphism (
14.4
).
∎

We list some immediate consequences of the construction of the isomorphism
\Phi
:

(1)

The isomorphism
\Phi
respects the perverse filtration on
\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}(\mathfrak{M}^{\Dol}_{r,0}(C),\mathbf{Q})
(respectively,
\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}(\mathfrak{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r},\mathbf{Q})
) induced by the morphisms
p_{\Dol}
(respectively
p_{\Betti}
).

(2)

There are natural inclusions
\ICA(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,0}(C))\subseteq\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}(\mathfrak{M}^{\Dol}_{r,0}(C),\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})
and
\ICA(\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r})\subseteq\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}(\mathfrak{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r},\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})
, and

\Phi(\ICA(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C)))=\ICA(\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d}).

Furthermore the isomorphism

(14.6)

\Phi\colon\ICA(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C))\rightarrow\ICA(\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d})

is the isomorphism induced by the homeomorphism
\Psi_{r,d}
.

We recall the definition of the
Hitchin morphism

(14.7)

\displaystyle\mathtt{h}_{r,d}\colon

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C)\rightarrow\Lambda_{r}\coloneqq\prod_{i=1}^{r}\HO^{0}(C,\omega_{C}^{\otimes i})

\displaystyle(\mathcal{F},\eta)\mapsto\mathrm{char}(\eta)=(\Tr(\eta),\Tr(\wedge^{2}\eta)),\ldots).

The target is a monoid: it records the supports of possible spectral curves (with multiplicity). The intersection cohomology
\ICA(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C))
carries a perverse filtration, defined with respect to the Hitchin morphism. Precisely, we set

\mathfrak{P}^{i}_{\mathtt{h}}\ICA(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C))\coloneqq\HO^{*}(\Lambda_{r},{}^{\mathfrak{p}}\!\tau^{\leqslant i}\mathtt{h}_{r,d,*}\mathcal{IC}(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C))).

The PI=WI conjecture of de Cataldo and Maulik
[
22
]
states that we have the identities

\Phi(\mathfrak{P}^{i}_{\mathtt{h}}\ICA(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C)))=W^{2i}\ICA(\mathcal{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d})

for all
i
.
By
[
21
]
the Borel–Moore homology
\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}(\mathfrak{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,d},\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})
carries a perverse filtration with respect to the morphism
p^{\Betti}
, while
\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}(\mathfrak{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C),\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})
carries perverse filtrations with respect to
p^{\Dol}
and
\mathtt{h}^{\sta}\coloneqq\mathtt{h}\circ p^{\Dol}
respectively. As in
[
20
, Sec.1.3]
we define a mixed perverse filtration

(14.8)

\mathfrak{F}^{i}\!\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}(\mathfrak{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C),\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})\coloneqq\sum_{j+k=i}\left(\mathfrak{P}_{p^{\Dol}}^{2k}\!\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}(\mathfrak{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C),\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})\right)\cap\left(\mathfrak{P}_{{\mathtt{h}}^{\sta}}^{j+2k}\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}(\mathfrak{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C),\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})\right).

To state the next theorem we restrict to slope zero Higgs sheaves, and (untwisted) representations of
\pi_{1}(\Sigma_{g})
. In
[
20
]
it is explained how to prove the following theorem in the presence of the nonabelian Hodge isomorphism (
14.3
).

Theorem 14.5
.

The following three statements are equivalent

•

The PI=WI conjecture is true.

•

There is an equality of filtrations of
\mathfrak{P}_{p^{\Betti}}^{0}\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}(\mathfrak{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,0},\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})

\Phi\left(\mathfrak{P}^{i}_{\mathtt{h}^{\sta}}\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}(\mathfrak{M}^{\Dol}_{r,0}(C),\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})\cap\mathfrak{P}^{0}_{p^{\Dol}}\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}(\mathfrak{M}^{\Dol}_{r,0}(C),\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})\right)=W^{2i}\mathfrak{P}^{0}_{p^{\Betti}}\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}(\mathfrak{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,0},\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})

•

There is an equality of filtrations of
\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}(\mathfrak{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,0},\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})

\Phi\left(\mathfrak{F}^{i}\!\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}(\mathfrak{M}^{\Dol}_{r,0}(C),\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})\right)=W^{2i}\HO^{\BoMo}_{*}(\mathfrak{M}^{\Betti}_{g,r,0},\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}}).

14.1.
\chi
-independence results

14.1.1.
Dolbeault side

Note that the target of the Hitchin map
\mathtt{h}\colon\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C)\rightarrow\Lambda_{r}
depends only on
r
and not
d
.
We start by recalling the following theorem of Kinjo and Koseki:

Theorem 14.6
.

[
46
]

Let
C
be a smooth complex projective curve of arbitrary genus. For all
d,d^{\prime}\in\mathbf{Z}
and all
r\in\mathbf{Z}_{>0}
there is an isomorphism of mixed Hodge module complexes

\mathtt{h}_{*}\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{r,d}^{\Dol,\mathrm{3d}}(C)\cong\mathtt{h}_{*}\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{r,d^{\prime}}^{\Dol,\mathrm{3d}}(C)

Taking derived global sections, we deduce that there is an isomorphism

\HO^{*}(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C),\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{r,d}^{\Dol,\mathrm{3d}}(C))\cong\HO^{*}(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d^{\prime}}(C),\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{r,d^{\prime}}^{\Dol,\mathrm{3d}}(C))

respecting the perverse filtrations induced by the Hitchin map
\mathtt{h}
.

We give
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathrm{MHM}(\Lambda))
the tensor structure
\boxdot
induced by the monoid structure
+\colon\Lambda\times\Lambda\rightarrow\Lambda
, i.e. we set

\mathcal{F}\boxdot\mathcal{G}\coloneqq+_{*}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{G}).

Comparing
d=0,1
in Theorem
14.6
and applying our main theorem:

Corollary 14.7
.

Let
C
be a complex projective curve of genus at least two. There is an isomorphism of complexes of pure mixed Hodge modules

\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot-\mathrm{Lie}}\left(\bigoplus_{r\geqslant 1}\mathtt{h}_{*}\underline{\mathcal{IC}}(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,0}(C))\right)\cong\bigoplus_{r\geqslant 1}\mathtt{h}_{*}\underline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathrm{vir}}_{\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,1}(C)}.

Taking derived global sections, there is a
\mathbf{Z}_{\geqslant 1}
-graded isomorphism

(14.9)

\operatorname{Free}_{\mathrm{Lie}}\left(\bigoplus_{r\geqslant 1}\ICA(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,0}(C))\right)\cong\bigoplus_{r\geqslant 1}\HO^{*}(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,1}(C),\underline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\mathrm{vir}}).

respecting the perverse filtrations induced by the Hitchin maps
\mathtt{h}
on both sides.

Remark 14.8
.

In particular, the cohomology of the moduli scheme of degree 1 Higgs bundles carries a Lie algebra structure via the isomorphism (
14.9
). Although there is surely a more down-to-earth way of writing it down, we are not sure what it is.

The Hodge-to-Singular correspondence of Mauri and Migliorini
[
54
]
(see also
[
55
]
) asserts that (after restricting to the reduced locus in
\Lambda
) the direct image
\mathtt{h}_{*}\mathcal{IC}(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C))
(for arbitrary
d
) decomposes into isotypic components of tensor powers of
\mathtt{h}_{*}\mathcal{IC}(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r_{i},0}(C))
(for decompositions
r=(r_{1},\ldots,r_{l})
). Corollary
14.7
gives a new interpretation of this fact, as well as showing that it extends over the whole of
\Lambda
. We finish this subsection with a final corollary, extending
[
54
, Corollary 1.9]
over the entire Hitchin base:

Corollary 14.9
.

Let
C
be a complex projective curve of genus at least two. Let
r\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geqslant 1}
and let
d,d^{\prime}
satisfy
\gcd(r,d)=\gcd(r,d^{\prime})
. Then there is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathrm{MHM}(\Lambda_{r}))
:

(\mathtt{h}_{r,d})_{*}\underline{\mathcal{IC}}(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d}(C))\cong(\mathtt{h}_{r,d^{\prime}})_{*}\underline{\mathcal{IC}}(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{r,d^{\prime}}(C)).

Proof.

Write
(r,d)=m(\overline{r},\overline{d})
and
(r,d^{\prime})=m(\overline{r},\overline{d}^{\prime})
where
m=\gcd(r,d)
. By Theorem
14.6
there is an isomorphism of complexes of mixed Hodge modules

\Sym_{\boxdot}\left(\bigoplus_{n\geqslant 1}\mathtt{h}_{*}\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{n\overline{r},n\overline{d}}^{\Dol}(C)\right)\cong\Sym_{\boxdot}\left(\bigoplus_{n\geqslant 1}\mathtt{h}_{*}\underline{\mathcal{BPS}}_{n\overline{r},n\overline{d}^{\prime}}^{\Dol}(C)\right).

Thus by (
13.3
) there is an isomorphism of complexes of mixed Hodge modules

\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot-\mathrm{Alg}}\left(\bigoplus_{n\geqslant 1}\mathtt{h}_{*}\underline{\mathcal{IC}}(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{n\overline{r},n\overline{d}}(C))\right)\cong\operatorname{Free}_{\boxdot-\mathrm{Alg}}\left(\bigoplus_{n\geqslant 1}\mathtt{h}_{*}\underline{\mathcal{IC}}(\mathcal{M}^{\Dol}_{n\overline{r},n\overline{d}^{\prime}}(C))\right)

and the result follows by comparing summands at
n=m
.
∎

14.1.2.
Betti side

We record the following Betti
\chi
-independence result

Theorem 14.10
.

Let
g\geqslant 2
. Then for
\aBPS^{\Betti}_{\mathrm{Lie},r,d}
as defined in (
14.2
), there is an isomorphism of cohomologically graded vector spaces

\aBPS^{\Betti}_{\mathrm{Lie},g,r,d}\cong\aBPS^{\Betti}_{\mathrm{Lie},g,r,d^{\prime}}

for all
d,d^{\prime}\in\mathbf{Z}
.

Proof.

From
\aBPS^{\Betti}_{\mathrm{Lie},g,\theta}=\operatorname{Free}_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\ICA(\mathcal{M}_{g,\theta}^{\Betti}))
,
\aBPS^{\Dol}_{\mathrm{Lie},\theta}(C)=\operatorname{Free}_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\ICA(\mathcal{M}_{\theta}^{\Dol}(C))
and the nonabelian Hodge homeomorphism (
14.6
) it follows that

(14.10)

\aBPS^{\Dol}_{\mathrm{Lie},r,d}(C)\cong\aBPS^{\Betti}_{\mathrm{Lie},g,r,d}

By
[
46
]
and §
13.3
there are isomorphisms

(14.11)

\aBPS^{\Dol}_{\mathrm{Lie},r,d}(C)\cong\aBPS^{\Dol}_{\mathrm{Lie},r,d^{\prime}}(C)

for all
d,d^{\prime}
, and the result follows.
∎

Note that if
(r,d)=1
, by definition

\aBPS^{\Dol}_{\mathrm{Lie},r,d}(C)\cong\HO^{*}(\mathcal{M}_{r,d}^{\Dol}(C),\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})\quad\quad\aBPS^{\Betti}_{\mathrm{Lie},r,d}(C)\cong\HO^{*}(\mathcal{M}_{g,r,d}^{\Betti},\mathbf{Q}^{\mathrm{vir}})

By the proof of the P=W conjecture
[
57
,
38
]
if
\gcd(r,d)=1
the isomorphism (
14.10
) carries the perverse filtration on the Dolbeault side to (twice) the weight filtration on the Betti side. Furthermore,
[
46
]
shows that the isomorphism (
14.11
) respects the perverse filtration for all
d,d^{\prime}
. It follows that for coprime
d,d^{\prime}
the isomorphism of Theorem
14.10
respects the weight filtration. We conjecture that this is in fact the case for
all

d,d^{\prime}
, regardless of coprimality. This would imply, amongst other things, the PI=WI conjecture (see
[
20
]
for details). Note that by
[
39
]
the E-polynomials (recording weights, but taking alternating sums over cohomological degrees) of
\aBPS^{\Betti}_{\mathrm{Lie},g,r,d}
and
\aBPS^{\Betti}_{\mathrm{Lie},g,r,d^{\prime}}
coincide. See
[
15
]
for details.

15.
Cuspidal polynomials of totally negative quivers

Let
Q=(Q_{0},Q_{1})
be a quiver. Ringel and Green defined in the beginning of the 1990s in
[
62
,
63
,
29
]
the
Hall algebra
of
Q
over a finite field
\mathbf{F}_{q}
as an algebra structure on the vector space having as basis the set of isomorphism classes of finite-dimensional representations of
Q
over
\mathbf{F}_{q}
:

H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}:=\bigoplus_{[M]\in\operatorname{Rep}_{Q}(\mathbf{F}_{q})/\sim}\mathbf{C}[M].

Green
[
29
]
defined a coproduct
\Delta
on
H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}
and Xiao
[
80
]
expressed the antipode, so that
H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}
has the structure of a twisted
5
5

5

The twist is explained in Xiao’s paper. It is not relevant here.
Hopf algebra. Sevenhant and Van den Bergh proved
[
74
]
that
H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}
has the structure of the quantized enveloping algebra of a Borcherds–Kac–Moody algebra with deformation parameter specialized at
\sqrt{q}
, where the generators are given by a basis of the space of
cuspidal functions

H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}^{\mathrm{cusp}}=\{f\in H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}\mid\Delta(f)=f\otimes 1+1\otimes f\},

satisfying Serre relations
6
6

6

They assume the quiver is loop-free. This assumption can be removed.
, see
[
31
, Theorem 3.4]
for a formulation.

For
\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}
, let
M_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)
be the number of isomorphism classes of
\mathbf{F}_{q}
–representations of
Q
of dimension
\mathbf{d}
;
I_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)
be the number of isomorphism classes of
indecomposable

\mathbf{F}_{q}
–representations of
Q
of dimension
\mathbf{d}
and
A_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)
be the number of isomorphism classes of
absolutely indecomposable

\mathbf{F}_{q}
–representations of
Q
of dimension
\mathbf{d}
. By Kac
[
42
]
, these counting functions are polynomials in
q
and moreover, by
[
35
]
(or
[
18
,
24
]
for different approaches), the coefficients of
A_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)
are nonnegative.

The graded character of the Hall algebra is given by the formula

\mathrm{ch}(H_{Q,F_{q}})\coloneqq\sum_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}M_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)z^{\mathbf{d}}=\Exp_{z}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{d}\neq 0}I_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)z^{\mathbf{d}}\right)=\Exp_{q,z}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{d}\neq 0}A_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)z^{\mathbf{d}}\right),

where
\Exp_{z}
and
\Exp_{z,t}
denote the plethystic exponentials, see
[
9
, Section 1.5]
. The second equality follows from the Krull–Schmidt property of the category of representations of the quiver and the third from Galois descent for quiver representations.

The space
H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}^{\mathrm{cusp}}
is naturally graded by the dimension vector:
H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}^{\mathrm{cusp}}=\bigoplus_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}^{\mathrm{cusp}}[\mathbf{d}]
. There has been a growing interest in understanding this space and to find a parametrisation of cuspidal functions
[
9
,
31
]
. The first step was to compute its dimension. We have the following result.

Theorem 15.1

(
[
9
, Theorem 1.1]
)
.

The dimension
\dim_{\mathbf{C}}H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}^{\mathrm{cusp}}[\mathbf{d}]
is given by a polynomial with rational coefficients
C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)\in\mathbf{Q}[q]
.

Bozec and Schiffmann combinatorially defined a new family of polynomials
(C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}^{\abso}(q))_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}
from the family
(C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q))_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}
, expected to enjoy more favourable properties:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}^{\abso}(q)=C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)\text{ if $\langle\mathbf{d},\mathbf{d}\rangle<0$},\\
&\Exp_{z}\left(\sum_{l\in\mathbf{Z}_{>0}}C_{Q,l\mathbf{d}}(q)z^{l\mathbf{d}}\right)=\Exp_{q,z}\left(\sum_{l\in\mathbf{Z}_{>0}}C_{Q,l\mathbf{d}}^{\abso}(q)z^{l\mathbf{d}}\right)\text{ if $\mathbf{d}\in(\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}})_{\prim}$ and $\langle\mathbf{d},\mathbf{d}\rangle=0$},\\
&C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}^{\abso}(q)=0\text{ else.}\end{aligned}\right.

The definition is motivated by the fact that if there exists a
\mathbf{N}\times\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}
-graded Borcherds Lie algebra
\mathfrak{n}_{Q}^{\mathbf{N}}
associated with the lattice
(\mathbf{Z}^{Q_{0}},(-,-))
, with graded character

\mathrm{ch}(\mathfrak{n}_{Q}^{\mathbf{N}})=\sum_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}A_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)z^{\mathbf{d}},

then the
\mathbf{N}\times\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}
-graded dimension of the space of simple roots is given by the generating series

\dim_{\mathbf{C}}\mathfrak{n}_{Q}^{\mathbf{N}}/[\mathfrak{n}_{Q}^{\mathbf{N}},\mathfrak{n}_{Q}^{\mathbf{N}}]=\sum_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}^{\abso}(q)z^{\mathbf{d}},

see
[
9
]
.

For totally negative quivers,
\langle\mathbf{d},\mathbf{d}\rangle<0
for every dimension vector
\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}
so that the two families of polynomials
(C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q))_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}
and
(C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}^{\abso}(q))_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}
coincide.

Theorem 15.2

(
[
9
, Theorem 1.4, Theorem 1.6]
)
.

The polynomials
C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}^{\abso}(q)
have integer coefficients. If
Q
is a totally negative quiver, they satisfy the equality

1-\sum_{\mathbf{d}>0}C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}^{\abso}(q)z^{\mathbf{d}}=\Exp_{q,z}\left(-\sum_{\mathbf{d}>0}A_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)z^{\mathbf{d}}\right).

The equality for totally negative quivers witnesses the fact that by the Sevenhant and Van den Bergh theorem, the Hall algebra of such a quiver is free, generated by the subspace of cuspidal functions.

Bozec and Schiffmann conjecture the following.

Conjecture 15.3
.

For any
\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}
,
C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}^{\abso}(q)\in\mathbf{N}[q]
.

This conjecture is known for isotropic dimension vectors
[
27
,
9
,
31
]
, but is open in general.

We fix now a totally negative quiver
Q
. Such quivers have no isotropic dimension vectors. As mentioned above, for such quivers the cuspidal polynomials
C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)
and absolutely cuspidal polynomials
C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}^{\abso}(q)
coincide. The case of general quivers will be the object of a subsequent paper. The theorem of Sevenhant and Van den Bergh
[
74
, Theorem 1.1]
implies that
H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}
is the free associative algebra having
\dim_{\mathbf{C}}H_{Q,\mathbf{F}_{q}}[\mathbf{d}]
generators in dimension
\mathbf{d}
.

Assuming that the coefficients of the polynomials
C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}^{\abso}(q)
are nonnegative, there exists a free
\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}\times\mathbf{N}
-graded Lie algebra
\mathfrak{n}^{\mathbf{N}}_{Q}
with the dimension of the
\mathbf{N}
-graded space of generators in dimension
\mathbf{d}
given by
C^{\abso}_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)
such that

(15.1)

\mathrm{ch}(\UEA(\mathfrak{n}^{\mathbf{N}}_{Q}))=\Exp_{q,z}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}\smallsetminus\{0\}}A_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)z^{\mathbf{d}}\right).

By the graded PBW theorem, this is equivalent to the equality

(15.2)

\mathrm{ch}(\mathfrak{n}_{Q}^{\mathbf{N}})=\sum_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}\smallsetminus\{0\}}A_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)z^{\mathbf{d}}.

Conversely, if such a free Lie algebra
\mathfrak{n}_{Q}^{\mathbf{N}}
exists, then the polynomials
C^{\abso}_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)
have nonnegative coefficients: they are given by the equality

(15.3)

\sum_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}^{\abso}(q)z^{\mathbf{d}}=\mathrm{ch}(\mathfrak{n}_{Q}^{\mathbf{N}}/[\mathfrak{n}_{Q}^{\mathbf{N}},\mathfrak{n}_{Q}^{\mathbf{N}}]).

By
[
19
, Section 1.2]
, the 3d BPS Lie algebra of §
13.1
,
\mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\aBPS}\coloneqq\HO^{*}\mathcal{BPS}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}^{\mathrm{3d}}
, is a
2\mathbf{N}_{\leqslant 0}
-graded Lie algebra with character

(15.4)

\mathrm{ch}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\aBPS})=\sum_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}A_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q^{-2})z^{\mathbf{d}}

which by Corollary
13.3
is isomorphic to
\aBPS_{\Pi_{Q},\mathrm{Lie}}
.

Via this isomorphism
\mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\aBPS}
is identified with the free Lie algebra with graded dimension of the spaces of generators given by

(15.5)

\mathrm{ch}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\aBPS}/[\mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\aBPS},\mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\aBPS}])=\sum_{\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}}\IP(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}},q)z^{\mathbf{d}},

the intersection Poincaré polynomial of
\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}}
.

Theorem 15.4
.

The Conjecture
15.3
is true for totally negative quivers:
C^{\abso}_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q)\in\mathbf{N}[q]
. Moreover, for any
\mathbf{d}\in\mathbf{N}^{Q_{0}}
,
\mathbf{d}\in\Sigma_{Q}
,

C_{Q,\mathbf{d}}(q^{-2})=\IP(\mathcal{M}_{\Pi_{Q},\mathbf{d}},q).

Proof.

This comes from the comparison of Equations (
15.3
) and (
15.5
), given that the character of the free Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}_{\Pi_{Q}}^{\aBPS}
is given by Equation (
15.4
), which is (
15.1
) up to the change of variables
q\leftrightarrow q^{-2}
.
∎
